These records support author review and source maintenance. They are not learner lesson content and must stay outside learner-facing exports.

## Record maintenance

Keep official sources mapped to lesson sections or claims, together with the revision reviewed, documentation-check date, limitations, and review status. A saved citation is not evidence of fresh verification. Update these records when technical content is checked or changed.

On October 19, 2026, the existing citations were relocated from both lessons. This was an editorial change, not a new documentation check, tenant test, learner-validation pass, or publication approval. Existing verification dates and notes are preserved below.

The fictional permissions and outcomes above are scenario assumptions, not names or guarantees supplied by SailPoint.

Use [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) to check how source systems are represented and connected. Compare the [access request process](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/index.html) with [provisioning monitoring](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) to distinguish a decision from its execution. ### Assessment intent for the current SYS-001 checkpoints

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning and acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | Appropriate access versus correct application execution | Verify the employee's continuing approved work and the removal requirement | A working application proves access remains justified |
| Case 2 | Business decision versus delivery | Approval is established; inspect the rejected operation and current target state | Another approval repairs a failed target change |
| Case 3 | Visibility versus a complete operating design | Collection is established; agree supported execution, ownership, and evidence | Importing accounts supplies a change mechanism |
| Case 4 | Scope of evidence | Limit conclusions to included applications; obtain separate evidence before judging excluded access | A completed review proves uncollected access appropriate or inappropriate |
| Support-application transfer | Connecting identity, permission meaning, decision, execution, and review | Explain supported automatic or owned manual delivery and continuing-access checks | Account creation alone fulfills identity governance |

The full questions and worked answers are in the learner lesson. These author fields are assessment metadata, not required inline labels.

For a closer check, compare the person-level description in [Identities Overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/index.html) with the matching behavior in [Account Correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html). ### Assessment intent for the current FND-001 checkpoints

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning and acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Question 1 | Identity/account cardinality | One identity is associated with three source records | Each account creates another person-level identity |
| Question 2 | Collection versus association | The account is collected; investigate its association and matching inputs | A missing identity relationship means the target account must be created |
| Question 3 | Separate attribute records and update mechanisms | Inspect identity mapping, collected account data, expected attribute sync, and execution evidence | Differing department values alone prove identity processing failed |
| Question 4 | Correlation versus account creation | Creation requires the supported provisioning operation and confirmed target outcome | Correlating an existing account creates an account elsewhere |
| Teach-back | Independent object and operation reasoning | Explain a new application example with separate population, association, creation, and attribute updates | Memorized labels establish understanding of the relationships |

## SYS-002

- Current lesson: [SYS-002](../lessons/SYS-002_Authentication_Authorization_and_ISC.md), revision 1.1.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Review method: read official sources for the mechanisms taught; distinguish general identity standards and security guidance from ISC product behavior.
- The finance and document applications, permission rules, account identifiers, event times, and outcomes are fictional teaching examples. No tenant or application test was performed.
- Author review completed for revision 1.0; learner validation and publication review remain pending.
- The existing SYS-001 and FND-001 source records retain their earlier verification dates. This new lesson's research does not constitute re-review of those lessons.

### Sources by lesson section and claim

| Official reference | Lesson section(s) and claim scope |
|---|---|
| [Authentication vs. authorization, Microsoft identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/authentication-vs-authorization) | What did the successful sign-in actually establish? Identity verification versus permission decisions; MFA; identity providers can apply access policies. |
| [Identity and access management fundamental concepts, Microsoft](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/fundamentals/identity-fundamental-concepts) | What did the successful sign-in actually establish?; Now follow Devi into the application. Supporting meanings of authentication, authorization, identity provider, MFA, federation, and SSO. |
| [What is single sign-on in Microsoft Entra ID?](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/enterprise-apps/what-is-single-sign-on) | Now follow Devi into the application. Participating applications and shared sign-in; SSO is not a blanket grant of application operations. |
| [Single sign-on SAML protocol, Microsoft identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/single-sign-on-saml-protocol) | Now follow Devi into the application. Request/response and assertion concepts; trusted origin, integrity, intended application, and validity are explained conceptually. Protocol-field details are outside this introductory step. |
| [Identity governance and administration, SailPoint](https://www.sailpoint.com/identity-library/identity-governance) | Where does ISC enter the picture? Governance and administration purpose; ongoing appropriateness of access. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Where does ISC enter the picture?; Follow the access change before following the sign-in. Configured account/access changes and automated versus manual fulfillment; operation support must be verified for the actual connector. |
| [Access request overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/index.html) | Where does ISC enter the picture?; Follow the access change before following the sign-in. Requestable items, configured approval, fulfillment, and request history. The example deliberately excludes future start dates and additional activation requirements. |
| [Identities overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/index.html) | Which records are we talking about? Person-level identity and associated accounts/access. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Which records are we talking about? Account correlation associates source accounts with identities; this is distinct from an application's sign-in mapping. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Which records are we talking about? Sources represent external systems and connectors integrate their account/access data. |
| [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html) | Which records are we talking about? Entitlements represent source access rights; the actual permission meaning comes from the application's rules. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Follow the access change before following the sign-in; Case 3. Account collection and scheduling; collection is separate from sign-in. No universal post-provisioning refresh timing is asserted. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Follow the access change before following the sign-in; What happens behind the screen?; Case 1. Manual tasks, provisioning reports, account activities, and operation errors. Simulated evidence is not represented as exact product output. |
| [Authorization Cheat Sheet, OWASP](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | What happens behind the screen? Trusted enforcement of protected operations; browser visibility alone is insufficient authorization. This is application-security guidance, not a claim about undocumented ISC internals. |
| [Revoke user access in an emergency, Microsoft Entra ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/users/users-revoke-access) | Put the two processes beside each other; What about removing access? Application sessions and tokens can outlive a central account/access change; effective revocation depends on application enforcement. No Microsoft-specific time interval is generalized to ISC. |
| [Configuring SailPoint as a service provider](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/config_isc_service_provider.html) | Does signing in to ISC change these distinctions? External-IdP sign-in to ISC is distinct from governing external systems. The lesson does not claim universal single logout or teach SAML configuration. |
| [User level permissions](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/user_levels.html) | Does signing in to ISC change these distinctions? Platform user levels govern actions within ISC; signing in alone is not administrative permission. |
| [Configuring access governance on SSO providers](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/govern_sso.html) | Does signing in to ISC change these distinctions? Supported integrations can govern accounts, app assignments, and entitlements on SSO providers; detailed application permissions still depend on the configured mapping. |

### Scope and applicability notes

- Sources currently use SailPoint Human Fabric / Identity Services branding. The course retains the user's ISC title. Do not infer that every capability in the current navigation is included in every ISC license.
- Microsoft sources support general identity and session concepts. They do not establish the behavior of every ISC-connected application or connector.
- No claim is made that ISC is the live decision service for every protected application operation. The example uses application-side authorization. Other architectures require their own verified explanation.
- The sign-in diagram is a conceptual successful path with selected failure branches, not a complete SAML protocol sequence or deployment architecture.
- The example's direct current-permission check is an explicit fictional application rule. Session snapshots and extra business-unit conditions are separately stated variations.
- No automatic session termination, fixed propagation delay, universal connector capability, or implicit tenant test is asserted.

### Reasoning exercise review

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Evidence and acceptable reasoning | Misconception addressed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | Approval versus delivery versus sign-in | Supplied integration error and missing target permission support a provisioning failure; inspect authorized integration privileges and verify any controlled retry | Resetting the user's password repairs an integration permission error |
| Case 2 | Provisioned account versus signed-in account | Account IDs differ; establish intended account and mapping before changing permissions | A correct operation on one account guarantees success for another |
| Case 3 | Observation time versus current state | The direct removal postdates collection; verify the reason, target state, and refresh path without automatic regrant | A collected account view is a live guarantee |
| Case 4 | Authentication strength versus access appropriateness | MFA and application enforcement can succeed while an inappropriate grant remains; investigate grant origin and business justification | Strong authentication proves that the account should hold its permissions |
| Transfer case | Reasoning with incomplete evidence | Verify current permission, enabled state, session account, and failed operation; if all supplied conditions hold, investigate the actual deployed rule | Approval and a reported operation result are complete proof of usable access |

The learner file contains the questions, supplied facts, and worked reasoning. These author notes record assessment intent; they do not replace the lesson.

Local link targets, source-link exclusion, and source/lesson synchronization are checked during the save review. No image renderer, tenant execution, or publication-format visual check is claimed.

## SYS-003

- Current lesson: [SYS-003](../lessons/SYS-003_Who_Owns_What_in_Identity_and_Access.md), revision 1.1.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Review method: official SailPoint documentation for product mechanisms and configured ownership behavior; separate author-developed operational guidance for team responsibilities and handoffs.
- Organizational responsibilities are a practical model to agree with each organization, not requirements imposed by ISC. Fictional directory-group mappings do not establish a real connector's capabilities.
- No tenant execution, learner validation, or publication approval has occurred.
- Earlier lessons retain their existing verification records; this research is not a new review of their complete content.

### Sources by lesson section and claim

| Official reference | Supported section or claim |
|---|---|
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | HR owns employment facts, but which facts reach ISC? Authoritative populations and configurable identity attribute mappings; Case 1. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | HR owns employment facts; Find the first disagreement. Account collection and evidence of what data reached ISC. The export incident is fictional. |
| [Configuring manager correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/manager_correlation.html) | The manager explains why; Find the first disagreement. Manager links, matching inputs, and retained prior correlation when a changed value cannot match a new manager. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | What does the ISC engineer own? Attribute/manager processing and the distinction between initiating access changes and proving provisioning success. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | HR owns employment facts; An ISC owner field isn't the same as a job title. Source/connector definitions and source ownership for assigned work. |
| [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html) | The application owner explains what the access permits; An ISC owner field isn't the same as a job title. Access rights, ownership designation, and display-description changes not altering target permissions. |
| [Configuring access requests for entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_entitlements.html) | The manager explains why; An ISC owner field isn't the same as a job title. Reviewer configuration and ownership roles; no universal manager approval or automatic in-flight rerouting is asserted. |
| [Access request overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/index.html) | Opening, handoff flow, and screen evidence. Requests, configured review, and subsequent fulfillment; approval evidence is not delivery evidence. |
| [Creating and managing governance groups](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/governance_groups.html) | An ISC owner field isn't the same as a job title. Groups of identities for governance work, distinct from target-system access groups. |
| [User level permissions](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/user_levels.html) | What does the ISC engineer own?; Where do security, support, and audit fit?; An ISC owner field isn't the same as a job title; Case 3. Platform administrative permissions are distinct from business ownership and job title. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Directory responsibility, manual fulfillment, and ownership. Supported account/access changes; documented manual entitlement task assignment to entitlement owner with source owner fallback. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Front-end evidence, task assignee inspection, and Case 4. Manual tasks, operation reports, and reported errors. |
| [Completing a certification campaign](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/certs/completing_campaigns.html) | Who owns the work after access has been granted?; Case 4. Review decisions, manual/automated remediation, and verification of removal. |
| [Audit reports and monitoring](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/audit-reports.html) | Where do security, support, and audit fit?; What does each screen actually show? Recorded events and reports support investigation; no universal evidence completeness or retention period is asserted. |

### Applicability and reasoning limits

- Current official pages use SailPoint Human Fabric / Identity Services names. The course retains the user's ISC title. Do not infer license entitlement from documentation navigation.
- The employee-request example assumes self-request, configured manager review, a valid manager-correlation design, and a supported directory write. The input and identity must be verified for the applicable configuration; full manager-correlation setup and source/profile precedence belong in their dedicated lesson.
- Reassignment of an existing request is an investigation requirement, not a claimed automatic side effect of changing a manager. Inspect current assignment and supported administration in the actual environment.
- The directory-to-application mapping and business-unit decisions are fictional. Their purpose is to distinguish delivery ownership from permission meaning; no real application behavior was tested.
- No source-owner designation is treated as a universal administrative grant. Actual assigned user levels, scope, and operation permissions need verification.
- The author-developed guidance on coordination, evidence handoffs, exceptions, and project agreements is recommended operating practice. It is not a statutory requirement, platform-enforced workflow, or promise of vendor response.
- Governance groups and directory groups are different object types; similar names imply no automatic membership synchronization.
- A completed access review does not prove a target removal. The illustrated manual task is expressly still open, with the target permission still present.

### Exercise intent and review

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning and acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | Data authority versus mapping responsibility | Correct input is already collected; inspect the wrong mapping, scope the correction, and verify resulting identity and downstream state | Change correct HR data to compensate for a configuration error |
| Case 2 | Delivered membership versus appropriate permission meaning | Application owner and specialists resolve meaning; directory/ISC teams coordinate any resulting membership and configuration changes | A successful technical operation proves access is appropriately designed |
| Case 3 | Owner designation versus administrative capability | Establish required work first; use authorized technical operators or justified scoped permissions | Every source owner needs broad administrative rights |
| Case 4 | Review decision versus removal execution | Actual assignee coordinates authorized operator; coordinator tracks evidence and escalation | Revoke selection or ticket transfer proves removal |
| Supplier-portal transfer | Shared responsibility with a read-only integration | Sponsor decides, register owner maintains facts, portal owner defines access, ISC configures supported manual work, vendor operator executes, coordinator verifies. Other named arrangements are valid when all responsibilities and evidence are covered | A vendor-owned application or read-only connector eliminates internal accountability |

The full learner-facing questions, facts, and worked reasoning are in the lesson. These notes record review intent and do not replace it.

The [editable handoff map](../assets/SYS-003_responsibility_handoffs.txt) is embedded verbatim as a text diagram. Its paths, labels, identifiers, and human verification boundaries were checked against the prose. File review checks source synchronization, local links, source-link exclusion, and the current/next lesson register. Publication rendering and learner interpretation remain unvalidated.

## SYS-004

- Current lesson: [SYS-004](../lessons/SYS-004_Intended_Known_and_Effective_Access.md), revision 1.1.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Review method: official product documentation for ISC records, collection, processing, and Search; Microsoft documentation for application-owned session boundaries; OWASP guidance for trusted authorization enforcement.
- The intended/recorded/effective distinction and investigation method are author-developed teaching models, not named ISC states or mandatory workflows.
- No tenant execution, beginner learner validation, or publication approval has occurred. All example accounts, times, permission names, and results are fictional.
- Earlier lessons retain their own review dates and limitations; this is not a new full technical review of their contents.

### Sources by lesson section and claim

| Official reference | Supported section or claim |
|---|---|
| [Working with identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/identity_mgmt.html) | First, make access specific enough to check. Identity information and associated accounts are distinct from the target account. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | What does ISC actually know? Source representation and connector integration; supported reading and writing are distinct capabilities. |
| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Definitions versus assignments. Single-source entitlement bundles and detection based on already-held entitlements; presence alone does not prove original provisioning provenance. |
| [Managing roles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/roles.html) | Role-based access and why applicable continuing assignments matter before treating a target-only correction as durable. No detailed overlapping-access removal rule is generalized. |
| [Access requests overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/user-help/requests/index.html) | What should Devi be allowed to do? Requests, configured review, and delivery are separate parts of the access process. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Activity records and change direction. Provisioning changes account/access state through supported automatic or manual arrangements. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Operation/task evidence and the fictional failed removal. Read the actual activity and operation outcome rather than inferring delivery from approval. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Collected observations, observation path, and coverage. Aggregation methods, scheduling, and filtering; collection is not a universal instantaneous live view. |
| [Managing accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html) | Collected account information and current target state are distinct. The documented need to aggregate for accurate account status supports checking observation freshness. |
| [Viewing identity control panels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html) | What does ISC actually know? Access-tab contents are represented access items, not a live application-operation test. |
| [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Fresh information can still be incomplete. Attribute organization, account schema, and represented access information. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Collection can cause configured processing; processing can initiate access changes but does not validate that provisioning succeeded. |
| [Search FAQs and sample data models](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/search/search_guide.html) | A fresh-looking screen can still contain older information. Search synchronization can lag; date queries use GMT and displayed dates use the browser time zone. No fixed maximum visibility delay is promised in the lesson. |
| [Searchable fields](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/search/searchable-fields.html) | Search scope and identity/account relationship limits. An empty identity search is not comprehensive evidence that a target account lacks access. |
| [Authorization Cheat Sheet, OWASP](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | What can the application actually allow? Trusted back-end authorization, not front-end button visibility, must decide protected operations. |
| [Revoke user access, Microsoft Entra ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/users/users-revoke-access) | Application-owned sessions and revocation conditions. This supports the need to verify application behavior, not a universal Microsoft timing or protocol model for ISC sources. |

### Applicability and reasoning limits

- Current SailPoint pages use Human Fabric / Identity Services naming. The requested ISC course title remains unchanged. Capabilities in site navigation are not assumed to be licensed or in scope.
- The reporting application is invented, not an undocumented real connector example. Its direct grants, current-permission checks, absence of alternate grants, and safe test results are stated assumptions.
- The 09:00 deadline is a business requirement. The lesson does not claim second-accurate application enforcement from a product expiry setting or a particular native expiration implementation.
- The first removal is an explicit target rejection without a completed removal. The later manual action is separately authorized and verified. Partial multi-operation success and uncertain outcomes require operation-specific inspection.
- The 09:20 collection is an illustrative observation event, not a guaranteed post-provisioning schedule. Search visibility is separately qualified.
- Suggested checks are operational reasoning, not a claim that every application exposes identical evidence or every ISC page has the same timestamp fields.
- Scope includes retained legitimate access, account identity, target permission meaning, application enforcement, and relevant session conditions. An absent search result or a service outage cannot substitute for authorization evidence.
- No generic automatic reconciliation action, universal retry safety, guaranteed incident closure procedure, or universal regrant behavior is asserted.

### Exercise intent and review

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning and acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | Observation age versus inspection time | Current target and operation evidence are needed; an older collection cannot settle a later removal's outcome | Opening the page after a change makes its data current |
| Case 2 | Business requirement versus configured intent | Inspect the profile against policy and assess shared impact before correction | Successful execution and matching records prove appropriate access |
| Case 3 | Negative Search result versus target reality | Establish account ownership/association, collection scope, and query/synchronization before taking action | Missing visibility requires another grant |
| Case 4 | Direct permission removal versus effective restriction | Investigate other grants and session behavior; distinguish individual operation success from overall requirement | Removing one grant proves all routes are closed |
| Two-account transfer | Scoped evidence and safe verification | Inspect both accounts, check grant/session conditions and collection coverage, and obtain meaningful authorization evidence without changing real bank details | One read-only account plus a service error proves an employee-wide restriction |
| Teach-back | Connecting the views without memorizing the scenario | Explain requirement, records and their time/scope, effective behavior, and the gap requiring investigation | The three views are product status labels or one interchangeable source of truth |

The complete questions, supplied facts, and worked answers remain in the learner file. The transfer answer accepts different check ordering when all material evidence gaps are addressed.

The [editable change and observation flow](../assets/SYS-004_access_change_and_observation.txt) matches the lesson's embedded text diagram. The diagram separates attempted delivery from collection and labels the human comparison. File checks cover exact text synchronization, local-link targets, revision and register consistency, and absence of source links or draft markers in the learner file. No graphical rendering, tenant execution, learner interpretation test, or publication-format validation is claimed.

## SYS-005

- Review method: official SailPoint documentation for product concepts and supported behavior; author-developed operational guidance for acceptance, handover, incident management, and change control.
- The four work categories are a practical model, not named ISC execution stages, mandatory departments, or a claimed formal service-management certification.
- No tenant execution, learner validation, or publication approval has occurred. The invoice system, permissions, connector check behavior, technical-account replacement, and recovery outcome are fictional.
- Earlier lessons retain their previous review scope and dates.

### Sources by lesson section and claim

| Official reference | Supported section or claim |
|---|---|
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Translate the requirement; acceptance and administration. Source/connector definitions and separate connectivity, aggregation, and provisioning verification. The specific connection-check behavior in the incident is fictional, not universal. |
| [Configuring sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/config_sources.html) | Integration scope and capability checks. Read-only connectivity is distinct from supported provisioning capability. No newer onboarding feature is assumed available. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Account correlation and the employee-number example. Matching associates source accounts with identities; the example is not a complete correlation algorithm or fallback explanation. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Aggregation, scheduled operation, and observation freshness. No particular frequency or completion-time guarantee is prescribed. |
| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Single-source entitlement bundle, request configuration, and deletion not deprovisioning previously held entitlements. Scope assumptions exclude competing assignments; detailed overlap behavior remains outside this lesson. |
| [Access overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/index.html) | Supporting role and entitlement concepts; access grouping is distinct from business decision authority. |
| [Access request overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/index.html) | Requestability, configured approval, and fulfillment. No universal manager review, instant grant, or supported automatic account creation is inferred. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Supported account/access changes and automatic versus manual delivery arrangements. The read-only portal variation requires a supported configured manual process. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Activity results, errors, and manual task tracking. An operation result and a completed assignment are not substituted for all required target evidence. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Screen action versus consequence. Processing evaluates configured access and can initiate changes, but completion does not validate target provisioning success. |
| [Completing a certification campaign](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/certs/completing_campaigns.html) | Governance follow-up and remediation verification. Current page is marked phased availability; lesson stays on review-decision/removal distinctions and does not assume a particular campaign UI, engine, or new feature is available in every tenant. |

### Applicability and operational limits

- Current documentation uses SailPoint Human Fabric / Identity Services names. Preserve the requested ISC course title, while checking naming, service enablement, and feature availability before publication.
- Acceptance criteria, runbooks, impact assessment, recovery coordination, and incident/problem distinctions are recommended operational reasoning, not product-enforced stages or a fixed organizational process.
- The example uses an existing enabled account, valid association, two requested permissions, configured manager review, and a supported write integration. No other assignment requires the permissions; no overlap/removal rule is generalized.
- The fictional incident explicitly supplies evidence that both writes were rejected before application. Other failures can be partial or uncertain; actual operation and target state must determine recovery.
- Replacing a technical account is the illustrated maintenance event. Credential rotation is separately defined; rotating a secret is not claimed to universally change privileges.
- No universal retry, cancellation, rollback, or incident-closure procedure is promised. Recovery must account for in-flight work, current access, authority, and supported behavior.
- Capability validation and safe test data are recommended. No tenant, connector, warehouse portal, or application test was executed while authoring.
- The manual-delivery variation does not imply that every read-only source automatically creates work tasks. Its configured support and ownership must be established.
- Review and request documentation includes availability notices. No UI navigation walkthrough, certification engine migration, or recently introduced request feature is taught here.

### Exercise intent and review

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning and acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | Design boundary versus operational fault | Confirm actual account absence, follow the agreed exception, or authorize a designed account-creation extension | Repeating a request supplies a missing capability |
| Case 2 | Decision authority versus operational execution | Resolve the conflicting instruction with the authorized owner, inspect current action state, and use supported change processes | An informal message cancels a review decision or in-flight operation |
| Case 3 | Individual recovery versus incident scope | Inspect all affected operations and target outcomes, including removals, before claiming complete recovery | The first user's successful retry proves service-wide recovery |
| Case 4 | Configuration rollback versus target remediation | Preserve evidence, stop further unwanted effects through controlled change, and separately remove excessive delivered access | Deleting an access profile undoes its past grants |
| Warehouse-portal transfer | Applying the whole service model to an uncertain integration | Agree requirements, verify capabilities or manual delivery, test boundaries, hand over operations, and coordinate later failure handling | Reading accounts or meeting a pilot date proves operational readiness |
| Teach-back | Relationships among work categories | Explain requirement, tested service, ongoing operation, support recovery, and feedback into decisions/design | Go-live ends implementation responsibility or governance starts only afterward |

The learner file contains the complete cases, supplied facts, and worked reasoning. Different task divisions are acceptable when decision authority, execution, operation, evidence, and follow-up remain covered.

The [editable service-work map](../assets/SYS-005_service_work_handoffs.txt) is embedded verbatim in the lesson. Its arrows describe human work/evidence handoffs and feedback, not automatic backend stages. Save checks cover diagram synchronization, source-link exclusion, local links, revision consistency, and current/next lesson records. Graphical rendering, publication-format validation, and beginner interpretation testing remain pending.

## SYS-006

- All named employee records, values, times, configured conditions, and application behavior are fictional. No tenant operation, source aggregation, provisioning, or application test was performed.
- This review concerns SYS-006. Other lessons retain their recorded verification dates and limitations.
- The enterprise map is an author-developed dependency and investigation aid, not a product architecture diagram or automatic diagnostic workflow.

### Sources by lesson section and claim

| Official reference | Supported section or claim |
|---|---|
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Put each record in its system. Source representation and connector integration differ from external target systems; connector capabilities and execution paths vary. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Collection into ISC and collection-gap variation. The current external value and the collected account observation need separate evidence. No fixed freshness or reprocessing guarantee is assumed. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Associations between collected accounts and the appropriate identity. The main example assumes verified associations and does not teach the full fallback or manual-correlation algorithm. |
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative population, per-attribute input mappings, multiple available sources, mapping preview, and controlled application of changes. Authority for department is an explicit business-design assumption. |
| [Automating role assignment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/role_assignment.html) | Criteria inputs, AND conditions, automatic assignment, and required access. The main example explicitly uses identity attributes rather than source-account criteria. |

| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Bundling source entitlements and selecting access on an existing account. The main case has exactly one reporting account; multiple-account selection details are outside its scope. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Mapped identity information and applicable role reevaluation; processing can initiate access changes but does not validate successful provisioning. No precise per-identity execution trace or schedule is invented. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Inspecting activity, account/recipient/source scope, errors, and results. Main and changed-evidence cases distinguish an unfulfilled assignment condition from an attempted rejected write. |
| [Synchronizing attributes](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/attr_sync.html) | A corrected identity department does not itself update directory department. Attribute sync is a separate configured write mechanism with eligibility and account conditions. |

| [Authorization Cheat Sheet, OWASP](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authorization_Cheat_Sheet.html) | Authentication versus operation/resource authorization; enforcement belongs in trusted application processing rather than front-end visibility alone. |
| [Revoke user access, Microsoft Entra ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/users/users-revoke-access) | Application-owned sessions and permission/reevaluation conditions need independent inspection. No Microsoft-specific expiry interval or universal application behavior is generalized. |

### Applicability and causal limits

- Official SailPoint documentation currently uses Human Fabric / Identity Services branding. The course retains the requested ISC name; site navigation does not establish a license or service entitlement.
- The example has an existing identity, verified source-account associations, an enabled correctly defined automatic role, supported provisioning, and one existing reporting account. The original role grant is absent and no alternate grant provides the permission.
- Employee number 16006, account rpt-16006, department FIN/OPS, and employment-status ACTIVE are fictional values. Employment status is an example identity attribute, not an asserted lifecycle-state/account-status equivalence.
- AND evaluation is reasoned from supplied values. The lesson expressly does not claim that a UI or event stream exposes each individual comparison.
- The diagram shows a currently selected directory input with HR also available. Mappings can select HR; both sources are not asserted to override the identity simultaneously.
- The approved attribute authority is HR in this organization. Selecting directory data is not inherently incorrect across all implementations; it conflicts with this supplied design.
- Recovery through a shared mapping requires impact assessment and authorization. Preview of a mapped value is not a target access test.
- Criteria mismatch explains the absence of a new grant from this configured path. No universal claim is made about all preexisting assignments, alternate requests, other grants, processing state, or activity-history completeness.
- The successful continuation is a fictional end-to-end result. No fixed timing, automatic post-write collection, guaranteed retry, or claim of tenant testing accompanies it.
- Correcting one dependency does not establish that every other component is healthy. Changed-evidence cases show where additional faults can exist.
- Network/connector deployment details and full attribute/role internals remain in their dedicated lessons. Their omission is identified rather than replaced by invented backend topology.

### Exercise intent and review

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning and acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | Identity attribute versus collected source-account value | Read the configured criterion input; inspect the mapping and applicable processing | The same attribute name on any record satisfies a criterion |
| Case 2 | Correct collected input versus wrong selection | Recollecting the unchanged HR input does not redirect the mapping; assess shared dependencies before correction | Aggregation universally repairs downstream access |
| Case 3 | Assignment versus delivery | Follow rejected write and current target state; investigate the actual delivery error | A role assignment is complete proof of usable access |
| Case 4 | Troubleshooting versus changing policy | Status-only criteria broaden the population; correct the input defect or obtain a new authorized requirement | Making one person qualify is sufficient justification for a shared criteria change |
| Contractor/location transfer | Reconstructing the map in another domain | Approved register WH2 reached ISC; mapping selects directory WH1; after scoped correction verify each downstream result and unintended population effects | Correcting an upstream value proves all target outcomes |
| Teach-back | Connected cause/effect explanation | State the supplying record, input selection, consuming condition, required operation, and evidence of effective use | A list of components explains their interactions |

The complete supplied facts and worked reasoning are in the learner file. The map method accepts different investigation starting points when identity, scope, time, and causal links are verified.

The [editable enterprise access map](../assets/SYS-006_enterprise_access_map.txt) is synchronized with the lesson's larger text diagram; its shorter criteria block is a separate teaching example. Diagram review covers selected inputs, false/true branches, write outcome boundaries, observation direction, and human checking. Save verification checks synchronization, link exclusion, local targets, and the current/next register. No graphical rendering, publication-format inspection, or beginner interpretation test has been performed.

The file's export time and import time are separate observations. No universal schedule, post-write aggregation, deletion behavior, or refresh interval is invented. |

Authoritative role is not connector write permission or exclusive authority for every attribute. |

The successful trace assumes a unique intended employee-number match. The empty-value case limits its conclusion to that comparison and retains applicable configured/default matching behavior. |

No universal task creation is inferred for every read-only connector or action. |

No blanket claim that every file change initiates a write. |

### Boundaries and assumptions retained in the teaching

- The finance example has one existing intended production account, an existing HR-established identity, a unique intended correlation match, properly represented permission values, and supported configured read and permission-add operations.
- Account identifier fin-20002 is a fictional target-account value, not an ISC source or identity object ID. Test and production can contain the same value without representing the same external record.
- The connector type is reusable integration behavior; the source supplies the instance's configuration and record context. A customized web-service integration still requires correct per-operation configuration.
- The finance success and credential-rejection variations are supplied outcomes, not results observed in a tenant. A successful read proves neither every write privilege nor target authorization for every user operation.
- Supplier-Portal-File is explicitly a read-only flat-file input, not a claim about every technology that can process files. A supported manual fulfillment path is conditional. Task routing does not confer application administrative rights.
- The 09:00/11:00/12:00 file case uses one fictional day and time zone; it illustrates data age, not a SailPoint timing guarantee.
- Source names do not prove environment or scope. Independent current target evidence and subsequent collection answer different questions. No automatic full collection, indefinite retry, immediate UI refresh, or session-revocation claim is made.
- VA/SaaS arrangements receive only enough explanation to avoid treating “direct” as a universal topology. VA clusters, network paths, IQService, and deployment mechanics remain in Module 3.
- Access applications/catalogs remain in Module 2.5. No rarely used connector feature inventory is added to an introductory object lesson.
- Current official documentation uses Human Fabric / Identity Services branding. The requested ISC course name is retained; navigation branding does not establish licensing or feature availability.

### Diagram and presentation record

Editable source: [FND-002 read/write boundaries](../assets/FND-002_read_and_write_boundaries.txt). Its text matches the lesson's inline text block. All diagram terms are explained before the diagram. READ is a successful selected account trace; WRITE is a separate conditional change path. Human follow-up is not represented as an automatic service dependency.

Local validation checks source equality, IDs, headings, links, and complete worked cases. Rendered live-site checks must separately verify the published text block, table wrapping, and lesson placement. Neither check is learner validation.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Test account | Environment and source context | Test observation does not prove production permission; obtain production account, operation result, and current target evidence. Either confirming delivery first or establishing the production account first is acceptable if the scope remains clear | Same connector and username mean the same external account | Sources; configuration; monitoring |
| Missing association | Collection versus correlation | Collection is established; missing employee number blocks that comparison, not every possible match. Examine association and all applicable matching behavior | Missing person-level association proves connector failure | Correlation; source account view |
| Old file | Data age and read/write direction | Export contains old observation; editing input is not a portal write. Obtain current portal evidence and refreshed export/import. Explain a real change path if a change is still outstanding | Successful import makes old data current or writes it back | Loading account data; provisioning |
| Rejected write | Operation-specific capability and privilege | Read/approval do not repair missing write authority. Investigate exact operation, configuration and permitted actions; authorize limited correction; use controlled retry/resubmission and verify result | Another aggregation or approval grants the integration write authority | Connector supported features; provisioning; monitoring |
| Deletion requirement | Requirement versus documented operation | Disable is not delete. Agree another supported fulfillment method or explicitly approve a changed requirement | Broad “account management” support covers every operation | Connector supported features; supplied hypothetical restriction |
| Unfamiliar-system transfer | Independent boundary and flow reasoning | Identify external records, source scope, connector method, population, association, required change method, and outcome evidence; naming missing information is valid | A connection label alone establishes a complete integration | All mapped sources within their stated limits |

Worked answers and supplied facts remain in the learner lesson. The metadata above stays outside the reading site.

| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Matching source accounts to identities is separate from simply carrying an employee-number attribute. Main example assumes verified associations; the full correlation algorithm is not re-taught here. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Available activity, errors, and operation-result evidence for the optional finance update. No single universal history is promised to expose every attribute evaluation. |
| [Identity control panels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html) | Identity attributes and represented access context. UI values are linked to their producing records/calculations, not treated as current live target queries. |

### Conditions and reasoning boundaries

- The employee moved from Operations to Finance. The opening observations are collected HR orgCode FIN, ISC identity department Finance, and collected finance-account department Operations. These are observations of different objects, not a contradiction requiring all stored strings to match.
- HR-Workers establishes the identity population through the Employees profile. The directory account is already associated and contains the work-email input. The lesson does not promise account-selection behavior when multiple accounts supply a mapped field.
- The identity department mapping deliberately converts the source code; the input remains FIN. Its conversion table is not a full transform configuration. Missing-input handling must be inspected, not inferred from an incomplete description.
- Optional attribute sync requires the supported existing-account write and eligible configured mappings. The diagram's final target value is a supplied successful continuation, not proof that an opening snapshot triggered or completed that update.
- A department-field write is distinct from group membership changes. No rule that department changes automatically confer every Finance entitlement is assumed.
- The group object has custom fields id/name/description/businessArea in this fictional integration. Account field groups and native entitlement type groups are deliberately aligned; the Entitlement setting and Type link are configured, and g-inv-read matches the designated Entitlement ID.
- The membership-link arrow is a reference relationship, not a provisioning instruction. Visible name FIN_INV_READ is not substituted for ID g-inv-read.
- Entitlement description and display-name behavior is limited to the documented ISC editing mechanism. No blanket guarantee is made about all metadata edits, source schema modifications, or external application changes.
- JSON is a teaching record, not an exact connector response, API request, or runnable command. Text versus Boolean, leading-zero codes, absent/null/empty values, lists, and date meanings are explained conceptually. Feature-specific type support and normalization must be checked.
- Null output from an identity-profile mapping can produce a null sync update under the applicable configuration. The actual target can have required-value restrictions, so the operation result still needs verification.
- No post-write full aggregation, immediate UI refresh, per-field trace screen, target-session behavior, or unperformed tenant test is claimed.
- Official documentation currently uses Human Fabric / Identity Services branding. The requested ISC course name remains; documentation navigation does not establish licensing or enabled features.
- Full transforms, identity-profile selection, attribute sync mechanics, data processing, and complex entitlement modeling remain in their primary lessons. This lesson supplies the supporting meanings needed for value tracing.

### Diagram and representation checks

- [Attribute value flow](../assets/FND-003_attribute_value_flow.txt): HR read, mapped calculation, optional successful configured sync, and separate human verification. Source code FIN is preserved; identity/target Finance follows the stated conversion/write conditions.
- [Membership-to-entitlement link](../assets/FND-003_membership_entitlement_link.txt): same source and configured groups type, matching reference value/Entitlement ID, and entitlement properties.
- Both text sources must match their inline lesson blocks exactly. The first diagram's arrows are conditional data/operation progression; the second is a reference link. Neither describes private service topology.
- Validate the JSON sample, record IDs, complete headings/paragraphs, table regions, and diagram blocks in local and published formats separately from beginner comprehension.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Different values, correct mapping | Account input, calculated identity output, separate target write | FIN-to-Finance is consistent with supplied mapping; determine expected finance behavior, sync eligibility, association, activity, and current target state before diagnosing failure | Different strings on separate objects prove identity or sync failure | Identity profiles; transforms; attribute sync; monitoring |
| Name versus identifier | Membership references and entitlement details | Compare returned values, connector mapping, account/type configuration, and designated ID; name agreement alone is insufficient; assess reference impact before correcting a schema | A readable matching name is equivalent to the designated reference ID | Account schemas; entitlement types |
| Missing code | Evidence limits and missing-input logic | Inspect actual transform/mapping and preview/processing evidence; incomplete description does not determine runtime result; investigate HR input | Blank means a particular default or guaranteed processing error | Identity profiles; transforms; processing |
| Apparent Boolean | Value representation and supported comparison types | Supplied input is text; inspect schema, collected value, conversions and consuming feature; do not infer final comparison result | The text false is already a Boolean false | Schemas; supplied example |
| Edited description | Descriptive metadata versus target changes | No source description write or membership removal follows from the documented edit; separate supported removal if needed | Rewording an entitlement changes who holds it or what the application enforces | Managing entitlements; provisioning monitoring |
| Chosen-field transfer | Independent object/value tracing | Identify owning object, source record, type/shape, input and configured calculation/write; predict only what the changed condition supports | A field label or memorized definition establishes the complete value flow | Mapped sources within their stated conditions |

Questions and worked answers remain in the learner lesson. Assessment metadata and official links remain outside the reading site.

## FND-004

- Lesson: [FND-004](../lessons/FND-004_Entitlements_Access_Profiles_and_Roles.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 2.4.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the introductory relationship scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 24004, inv-24004, dir-24004, Invoice-System, Corporate-Directory, permission values, role/profile names, assignment conditions, and outcomes are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending; working-edition deployment is a separate state.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html) | Entitlements represent source access; they can be grouped in profiles or included directly in roles. Real effects and supported types come from the external system and connector, not an ISC display name. |
| [Access overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/index.html) | Single-source access-profile purpose and access-management context. The overview's older role wording is not used to exclude directly included entitlements documented by Managing Roles. |
| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Single entitlement source and at least one entitlement; profile detection from held contents; applicable automatic multiple-account criteria; deletion does not deprovision previous entitlements and requires removing provisioning references. Main example has one account per source and no activation-dependent access. |
| [Managing roles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/roles.html) | Provisioning service required; standard role contents can include access profiles and direct entitlements across sources; configured ownership differs from holding access; applying changes; retained overlapping entitlements through another assigned role; disabling/deleting a role does not deprovision previous access. |
| [Automating role assignment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/role_assignment.html) | Criteria, identity-list and request paths, enabled role and applicable processing, AND semantics, automatic criteria removal, and account retention after entitlement revocation. The supplied criteria illustrate logic rather than a complete production eligibility policy. |
| [Managing requests for roles and access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Requestability and configured review; request-based role assignment can serve users outside automatic assignment criteria. No universal manager approval is asserted. |
| [Configuring entitlement requests](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_entitlements.html) | Supporting requestability of applicable individual entitlements; no blanket assertion that every entitlement is requestable or every tenant has the required service/configuration. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Applicable processing evaluates role assignment and can initiate access changes; processing does not validate successful provisioning. No exact schedule, per-comparison trace, or fixed operation order is taught here. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Separate account/access change fulfillment. Main example uses existing accounts and supported configured writes; no automatic account creation, retry policy, or universal transaction behavior is inferred. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Activity/source/account/recipient scope, operation results, errors, and target-evidence investigation. A recorded role assignment is not proof of every target outcome. |
| [Identity control panels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html) | Identity access views represent roles, profiles, and entitlements; represented access does not prove original grant history or current live application enforcement. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) and [account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Supporting source/connector/account distinction and verified identity associations. Native application roles can be represented as source entitlements only where the connector and configuration support that access type. |
| [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Supporting distinction between population/identity-attribute configuration and a single-source access bundle. Full profile and mapping mechanics remain outside this introductory access-model lesson. |

### Conditions and reasoning boundaries

- Devi has employee number 24004, department Finance, and jobCode AP_READER. Her existing finance and directory accounts are correctly collected/correlated, with exactly one relevant account on each source.
- InvoiceRead and SupplierRead belong to Invoice-System and make up the Invoice reading access profile. FinanceWorkspace belongs to Corporate-Directory and is included directly in Accounts payable reader. The combined role spans sources without changing the profile's single-source boundary.
- These source values and external effects are fictional. The role/profile labels do not create correspondingly named native target objects, and adding direct role content does not bypass fulfillment controls.
- The role is enabled with prepared access/assignment configuration and the required Provisioning service. The simplified identity criteria require both department Finance and jobCode AP_READER; they are not a complete safe production policy for every worker population.
- Main-path access is ordinary provisioned access, not activation-dependent entitlement access. Dynamic-role dimensions and Just-In-Time details remain outside the opening object map.
- There are no preexisting relevant grants, alternate assignments, or competing access requirements in the main successful example. The detection and overlap variations explicitly change those facts.
- Automatic assignment, identity-list membership, and configured access requests are different supported paths. Requestability does not establish approval or delivery. No native role-name/job-title similarity is used as an assignment rule.
- The delivery diagram shows required account operations, not guaranteed concurrency, a source order, an atomic cross-system transaction, or universal rollback/retry behavior.
- Partial outcome: FinanceWorkspace and InvoiceRead are present; SupplierRead is rejected due to operation authority. This is supplied fictional evidence, not an observed tenant test or general claim about connector error handling.
- Access-profile detection can recognize an already-held bundle during processing. That representation does not prove a request or the original grant source, nor establish assignment of the broader ISC role.
- Multiple-account criteria are mentioned only for the documented applicable automatic access-profile path. They are not generalized to direct role entitlements or request-based account selection.
- Another assigned role retaining overlapping InvoiceRead is the documented overlap case. The lesson does not infer one universal removal rule for all direct, manual, requested, or inherited grants.
- Criteria-based revocation of an automatic role assignment differs from disabling or deleting the role definition. The latter does not remove previously granted access. Access-profile deletion also is not entitlement deprovisioning.
- Entitlement revocation does not delete accounts. Account creation/deletion and session behavior are separate conditions; no immediate UI refresh or automatic complete post-write aggregation is promised.
- Current official branding uses Human Fabric / Identity Services. The requested ISC course name remains; navigation branding does not establish service entitlement or license.
- Detailed entitlement modeling, access profiles, roles, request/removal mechanics, and overlap analysis remain in their primary modules. This lesson supplies an independently understandable introductory object and flow map.

### Diagram and review checks

- [Access-object relationship tree](../assets/FND-004_access_object_relationships.txt): role contains one single-source profile and one directory entitlement directly. Branches mean contents, not execution.
- [Assignment conditions](../assets/FND-004_assignment_conditions.txt): department/jobCode comparison and AND relationship; no invented hidden evaluation trace.
- [Assignment and delivery](../assets/FND-004_assignment_and_delivery.txt): identity inputs, applicable assignment evaluation, separate source-account changes, results, and human verification.
- All three text assets must match the inline text blocks exactly. Membership conditions, employee/account IDs, source boundaries, and worked answers are reviewed together.
- Local and rendered-site checks verify complete source text, headings, diagrams, tables, and navigation. Neither source matching nor deployment is beginner learner validation.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Two-source package | Access-profile boundary versus role composition | Keep a single-source finance profile; combine through a role with direct directory entitlement or useful separate directory profile | A broad package name allows a profile to contain entitlements from multiple sources | Access profiles; roles |
| Similar job title | Definition name versus assignment inputs | AP_MANAGER does not meet the stated AP_READER condition; inspect other assignment/access evidence separately | A similar job title or role label proves automatic assignment | Role assignment; request configuration |
| Detected profile | Recognition versus historical delivery | Existing held contents can be detected; inspect prior grants/approvals separately, not inferred from represented profile | Seeing a profile proves ISC originally provisioned it | Access profiles; identity processing |
| Partial delivery | Focused source/account/operation investigation | SupplierRead on inv-24004 is the failed branch; inspect rejection and authorize limited correction/retry; approval does not provide target operation authority | An assigned role or successful neighboring operation proves the entire requirement was delivered | Monitoring; provisioning |
| Overlapping role | Remaining valid requirement versus failed removal | Inspect the other assigned role and target state; retained overlapping access is not automatically a failure | Removing one role must erase every entitlement it contained | Roles, revoking entitlements with role changes |
| Deleted definition | Object maintenance versus revocation | Profile deletion/role disablement is not deprovisioning; inspect intended revocation path, remaining grants, activity and target result | Deleting or disabling an access object removes previous target access | Roles; access-profile deletion |
| Two-system transfer | Independent access design and evidence | Explain useful single-source bundles, cross-source role content, recipients, accounts, fulfillment, preexisting/rejected/overlap variation; direct entitlements are acceptable where useful | Memorizing a three-layer stack supplies a complete integration or evidence model | All mapped references within stated scope |

Learner facts and worked answers stay in the lesson. Assessment intent, limitations, and official links stay in author records.

## FND-005

- Lesson: [FND-005](../lessons/FND-005_Sources_Access_Applications_Catalogs_and_Sign_In.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 2.5.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 25005, accounts dir-25005/exp-25005, ExpenseHub, Corporate-Directory, group meanings, profile/application names, sign-in mapping and operation requirements are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Configuring access applications](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/app-config.html) | Business-facing access applications versus enterprise systems; grouping source access profiles; account-source configuration; multiple access applications for one source; enabling/visibility/request controls; password-management context; deletion retains assigned access profiles. The directory-backed expense design is a fictional realization of the documented pattern. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Source, connector, enterprise application and access application meanings; representation/integration boundaries. An access application is not a new connector or proof of native application account collection. |
| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Single-source entitlement bundle and example one-entitlement profiles. Main request concerns one existing directory account; no multiple-account selection algorithm is generalized. |
| [Managing requests for roles and access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Requestability, selected profile, configured review, independent profile request route, segmentation-related profile visibility, and already-held-access restrictions. Exact legacy tab labels are not taught as a universal current UI. |
| [Working with access requests](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/user-help/requests/request_center.html) | Requester/recipient context, offered roles/profiles/entitlements, selecting a profile under an application. The page includes legacy instructions; this lesson teaches the object and process relationships without repeating its click sequence. |
| [Managing access request segments](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/segments.html) | Segment audience is based on requester visibility; visible items can be requested for other recipients the requester is authorized to request for. Items outside segments can remain broadly requestable; org-admin visibility is broader. Segmentation is not a substitute for recipient eligibility/approval rules or runtime authorization. No exact sync delay or segment-size limit is generalized. |
| [Adding access applications to password management](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/pwd/add_access_app.html) | Supporting source-account/password-management purpose. A business-facing app name does not establish a separate native application password, a group grant, or equivalence among account source, authentication source, and application sign-in configuration. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) and [monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Approval is separate from the supported directory membership change; request/account/source result evidence and current target observation are distinct. No guaranteed retry, post-write aggregation, or atomic cross-system fulfillment is asserted. |
| [Authentication versus authorization, Microsoft identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/authentication-vs-authorization) | Authentication, IdP, SSO, and application operation authorization are distinct. This is primary identity-platform guidance, not a requirement to use Microsoft or a claim that ISC access applications configure the IdP trust. |
| [SAML single sign-on protocol, Microsoft identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/single-sign-on-saml-protocol) | Supporting separate application/IdP sign-in exchange and trusted information. The lesson does not teach protocol fields, mandate SAML, or imply every sign-in requires a new password prompt. |
| [Revoke user access, Microsoft Entra ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/users/users-revoke-access) | Application-owned session and reevaluation boundaries. No Microsoft token lifetime, session-revocation behavior, or universal immediate refresh rule is generalized to the fictional ExpenseHub application. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Matching a source account to an ISC identity is distinct from the external application's separate sign-in-to-local-account mapping. Main directory association is explicitly correct. |

### Conditions and reasoning boundaries

- Devi already has a directory account and a separate ExpenseHub-native account. ISC collects/correlates the directory account only in this example. No ExpenseHub source, automatic native-account creation, or native-permission inventory is assumed.
- Submit expenses contains EXP_SUBMIT; Approve expenses contains EXP_APPROVE. Both are directory entitlements with fictional business effects. Selecting one profile does not select every profile grouped under the access application.
- The Expenses account source is Corporate-Directory because directory membership is the chosen access-control input. A separate Knowledge grouping can use another directory-backed profile without creating another directory.
- Catalog means discoverable request offerings, not a mandatory new object named Catalog or an inventory of all existing enterprise access.
- Main path: requester and recipient are both Devi, request visibility/configuration is valid, no EXP_SUBMIT is held initially, and exactly one directory account is intended. Required review succeeds and the supported write succeeds in the selected continuation.
- Requester-based segmentation is explicitly distinguished from recipient eligibility. A Finance approval policy for recipient access must consider the actual recipient, rather than infer eligibility from the requester's catalog audience.
- No assumption that creating a segment restricts all otherwise unsegmented items, or that an administrator sees the same catalog as an ordinary requester. Other request conditions still apply.
- SSO trust and account matching are separately configured and already working for the main trace. The IdP receives the relevant current group membership and ExpenseHub receives/accepts current trusted sign-in information. These are supplied conditions, not live observations or automatic timing guarantees.
- ExpenseHub's submit operation requires EXP_SUBMIT information, an enabled intended account, and its other requirements. Success is an explicit fictional outcome. No universal group-to-app permission behavior is claimed.
- The wrong-account case changes the application's sign-in mapping, not the already correct ISC directory association. Recovery requires the relevant authorized owners and target outcome checks.
- Existing sessions may not immediately use changed group information. Catalog deletion, access revocation, membership changes, and session termination are separate actions.
- Local native privileges outside the directory groups are outside this source's observed data. An access application's business name does not fill that visibility gap.
- Hiding a grouping is not proof of target revocation or absence of all independent profile-request routes. Deleting an access application retains assigned profiles; external software/accounts remain separate objects.
- Current docs use Human Fabric / Identity Services branding. ISC remains the requested course name. Features, enabled request experience, and password-management support require the actual tenant/connector configuration.
- Legacy single-sign-on app settings are deliberately not taught.
