The current ISC Launchpad documentation describes interactive processes and Just-In-Time activation; it is not used here as a generic SSO application-portal assumption. The example's sign-in portal belongs to the organization's IdP arrangement.
- Detailed request, segment, password, SSO, token, and session configuration remain in primary lessons. This lesson explains the required meanings and connected boundaries without a UI-click or protocol-field inventory.

### Diagram and review checks

- [Application/source map](../assets/FND-005_application_source_map.txt): enterprise system, access application, configured account source, selected access profiles, and directory entitlement values. Branches are grouping relationships, not writes.
- [Request and sign-in paths](../assets/FND-005_request_and_sign_in_paths.txt): recipient request, approved configured directory update, separate IdP/application sign-in, existing native account match, and runtime operation decision.
- Both assets must match the corresponding inline blocks. Employee/account values, single-source profiles, and access meanings agree with the examples and worked answers.
- Local and published checks separately verify complete text, headings, diagrams, tables, and navigation. Neither is a beginner comprehension test.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Directory-backed Expenses | Enterprise system versus technical integration and logical grouping | Names do not prove error; validate directory group control, intended account, and application use of group data; native visibility still separate | Every business app must use an identically named direct source | Access applications; sources |
| One selected profile | Grouping versus requested item and delivered membership | Submit request concerns EXP_SUBMIT, not EXP_APPROVE or every offered package; inspect recipient and actual write | Approval for an application means all its grouped permissions were granted | Request configuration; request user help; monitoring |
| Different requester/recipient | Catalog segmentation versus recipient-related authorization | Requester visibility can allow requests for authorized other recipients; design eligibility/review against recipient requirements | Recipient outside the requester's segment must automatically be blocked by segmentation | Access request segments |
| Wrong application account | Separate matching relationships | Correct ISC directory association does not repair ExpenseHub's sign-in mapping; inspect authorized configuration and intended native-account/current access evidence | Repeating ISC correlation repairs every external sign-in identity mismatch | Correlation; identity-platform guidance |
| Deleted application grouping | Presentation/object removal versus access revocation | Assigned profiles can remain; establish supported removal and target/session evidence separately if that is the requirement | Deleting a catalog grouping revokes its target access and ends sessions | Access-application deletion; provisioning; session guidance |
| Business application transfer | Independent boundary and evidence reasoning | Identify external system/accounts, technical source/data scope, request grouping/item/recipient/change, independent sign-in and operation checks; name the fault boundary | A request card or app name identifies the entire technical path | All mapped references within stated scope |

Questions and worked answers stay in the learner lesson. Source references, limits, and assessment metadata stay outside the reading site.

## FND-006

- Lesson: [FND-006](../lessons/FND-006_Object_IDs_Account_Identifiers_and_References.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 2.6.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 26006, Ledger, login/native keys, account and entitlement data, warehouse transfer, and all technical-ID stand-ins are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is a separate presentation check.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Account model, current SailPoint Python SDK](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/python/accounts/models/account/) | Account object id, source_id, optional identity_id, source name, native_identity, attributes, and separate schema-determined uuid. SDK property spelling differs from JSON camelCase. This is a field-meaning comparison, not a runnable request or promise that all interfaces expose identical fields. |
| [Get an account, current API documentation](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/get-account-v-1/) | Account field descriptions checked through indexed official API text and the rendered official SDK model; the fetched endpoint page itself is client-rendered and did not expose the property descriptions in its initial HTML. No tenant call or endpoint execution is claimed. |
| [Searchable fields](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/search/searchable-fields.html) | Identity/manager/owner technical IDs; account id/name/accountId distinctions; source IDs; role/profile/entitlement references. In the account context, accountId uses source aiqAccountName if available, otherwise account display name. Search semantics are not generalized to the account API. |
| [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Designated Account ID/Account Name versus technical object ID; documented warning about changing identification designations after aggregation; entitlement-marked account attribute, matching type name, and matching designated entitlement ID value for membership/detail linking. Exact source identifier mappings remain connector-specific. |
| [Entitlement types](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/entitlement_types.html) | Entitlement schemas and designated ID/Name; changing designated attributes after aggregation can create duplicate entitlements. The lesson distinguishes schema selection changes from external value changes and display-label editing. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Configured account/identity attribute comparisons and resulting account association. Employee number 26006 is an explicitly fictional successful one-identity match, not a universal matching rule or claim that business identifiers are automatically technical IDs. |
| [Entitlement model](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/python/entitlements/models/entitlement/) | Separate entitlement id, name, attribute, value, source schema object type, source reference, and source attributes. E-26 is a non-runnable teaching stand-in; g-41 is a fictional source value. |
| [Access profile model](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/python/access-profiles/models/access-profile/) | Profile id, name, source reference, and entitlement references. The lesson does not adopt endpoint-specific requestability defaults or give API payloads. |
| [Access profile document model](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/python/v2025/models/access-profile-document/) | Read/search representation also separates profile ID/name, source, and entitlements. Used as supporting object-model evidence, not as a current endpoint contract. |
| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) and [roles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/roles.html) | Single-source profile and role/profile relationship. R-26 illustrates inclusion, not a requirement that all roles only use profiles, an assignment to Devi, or proof of fulfilled access. Detailed mechanics were checked for FND-004 and are not re-taught here. |
| [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html) | Editing an ISC entitlement's display name/description does not send those edits to the source. Display refresh and object identity are distinct; no universal instant Search refresh is promised. |
| [Snowflake account and entitlement IDs](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/snowflake/help/integrating_snowflake/identity_attributes.html) | Concrete connector-specific counterexample: source user/role name values are identifier values and the connector warns against changing them after aggregation. Not a universal rule for all connector renames. |
| [Account workflow trigger examples](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/workflows/workflow-triggers.html) | Official examples separate account id, name, nativeIdentity, uuid, and source information, including directory distinguished-name native identities. Used to validate the identifier distinction, not to introduce workflow execution or guarantee event ordering. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) and [monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Supporting distinction between identifying the intended account/access, attempting a supported change, reported result, collected observation, and current target access. Prior course verification retained; no new tenant execution or universal ID-to-write pipeline asserted. |

### Conditions and limits

- All short letter-number IDs explicitly stand in for real technical IDs. They are not valid-format examples to submit to an endpoint. Login, employee number, native key, and ISC record IDs have distinct meanings.
- Ledger's native key u-778 and login fin-26006 are deliberately different. The example asserts that Ledger keeps its key during the login rename; this is not generalized to actual connectors or all schema identifier fields.
- The lesson does not claim a universal one-to-one mapping between nativeIdentity, uuid, Account ID, Account Name, and account object id. Actual connector/schema data must establish the mapping. Account field spellings are interface-specific.
- Correlation compares configured attributes. It is separate from identifying an account within the source, authentication, account creation, and target fulfillment.
- Membership matching uses source/type/schema context and the designated source ID value, not the ISC entitlement object ID or an arbitrary display label.
- Same labels and copied native values across test/production do not establish the same object. A valid reference can still select the wrong business environment.
- Display-name editing in ISC is distinguished from changing a schema designation, changing an external identifier value, and deleting/recreating an object. No fixed duplicate, cleanup, retry, or automatic-reference-repair behavior is invented for all such changes.
- E-27 deletion followed by a current record E-28 is a supplied fictional before/after fact, not a universal claim about every entitlement reset or collection cycle.
- Relationship diagrams do not represent SailPoint private storage, an execution pipeline, assignment origin, live synchronization, or current usable access.
- Owner and manager identity references identify person-level records; responsibility does not itself grant the represented access.
- Source IDs, identity/account details, and access data are not credentials or authorization grants, but still require approved handling. No credential or live tenant evidence is included.

### Diagrams and author checks

- [Account/identity references](../assets/FND-006_account_identity_references.txt): account object to source and correlated identity, with external-account context shown separately.
- [Access-definition references](../assets/FND-006_access_definition_references.txt): role/profile/source/entitlement configuration and the separate account-membership observation.
- Both editable text files match the inline lesson blocks. IDs, native keys, membership values, source contexts, and arrow explanations agree with the prose and answers.
- Local and live publication checks verify full text, headings, tables, diagrams, and navigation separately from learner validation.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Correct recipient ID | Object type/context versus business or account identifier | Use intended tenant's resolved identity ID I-26, not employee number or account IDs | Any value called Devi's ID is interchangeable | Account model; Search fields |
| Matching labels, wrong environment | Reference resolution and business correctness | Resolve profile/source/entitlement, recognize test versus production, inspect actual operation; don't retry the wrong design | A matching label proves correct access | Profile model; entitlement model; profile source constraint |
| Changed entitlement label | ISC label versus source permission | E-26 remains g-41 on S-26; label edit is not sent to Ledger and is not a new grant | Renaming an ISC display name renames or replaces the source group | Entitlement editing |
| Friendly words in membership | Source ID matching and schema dependencies | Compare raw collected value with designated ID; locate export/mapping/data error before changing schema | Labels can substitute for configured membership IDs | Account schema linking; entitlement types |
| External login rename | Native identifier stability is connector-specific | Distinguish retained key from identifier-value change; verify schema, returned records, association and operation evidence | Every rename either always preserves or always breaks all references | Account model; schema warning; Snowflake counterexample |
| Warehouse transfer and teach-back | Independent tracing across objects and systems | Follow P-81/S-81/E-81 and intended A-81/native w-305; reject E-82 substitution; a label-only edit is not delivery evidence | Same label/value makes cross-source references interchangeable | All mapped references within stated scope |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Reference evidence, test intent, and review limitations remain outside it.

## FND-007

- Lesson: [FND-007](../lessons/FND-007_One_Identity_with_Multiple_Accounts.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 2.7.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 27007, People HR/Corporate Directory/Stockroom, account logins and IDs, usage values, access meanings, operation outcomes, and payroll transfer are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is a separate check.
- Authoring milestone: all seven Module 2 lessons exist as complete authored files; this does not establish beginner comprehension or completion of the whole course.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Multiple rows with one associated identity can represent multiple accounts on the same source, with different access. Removing an ISC account representation does not delete the external account; it can return during complete aggregation. No universal duplicate-account cleanup action is advised. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Configured attribute comparison and account association; uncorrelated accounts have not matched an authoritative identity. Two correct personal-account associations do not merge accounts, and an incorrect association is not the same as absent correlation. The employee-number match is a fictional valid-data condition, not a universal key or multi-identity tie-breaker. |
| [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative population, identity creation configuration, and person-level attribute mappings. Multiple non-authoritative Stockroom accounts are not treated as duplicate authoritative identities or a reason to apply an identity merge. |
| [Account Attribute transform](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/) | Distinct value-lookup mechanism: specified source/attribute, default oldest-account order by created date, non-null fallback, and filter/sort/first-account controls. This default is explicitly not generalized to access-profile provisioning. No executable configuration or blanket claim about all identity mappings is supplied. |
| [Managing access profiles: Multiple Account Options](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html#multiple-account-options) | Automatic role/lifecycle profile provisioning can use account criteria; single-account exclusion; no/multiple matches fail applicable provisioning; manual source-owner selection requires enablement and can need separate manual fulfillment. Daily/Admin values are fictional source data. No universal first/oldest/main-account selection or direct-role-entitlement algorithm is invented. |
| [Automating role assignment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/role_assignment.html) and [lifecycle states](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/lifecycle.html) | Supporting person-qualification versus account-targeting distinction and automatic profile-assignment contexts. Role and lifecycle mechanisms are introduced only to explain where this account selection applies; full role/lifecycle configuration remains in primary lessons. |
| [Managing requests for roles and access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Requester selects intended account for recipients with multiple source accounts; profile association only once per identity prevents a repeat request for a second account. Entitlement requests or role bundling are documented alternatives, not bypasses of eligibility/review/account targeting. |
| [Request Center user help](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/user-help/requests/request_center.html) | Supporting requester/recipient and requested-item context; account selection does not equal approval or successful fulfillment. Legacy click sequences are not taught as universal current UI behavior. |
| [Managing accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html) | Account-level enabled/disabled state and supported operations; external deletion is distinct from representation removal. Documented continuing-role account recreation is the reason to inspect assignments before deletion. No claim that a collected disabled flag terminates all application sessions. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Associated account/source, attempted changes and results, and manual task details. A successful operation on the selected wrong account can fail the business requirement; actual target and later collected observations remain distinct evidence. |
| [Viewing identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html) | Person-level access/activity presentation and related account context. The example explains represented data rather than promising specific UI columns, a complete live target query, or licensed graph functionality. |

### Conditions and limits

- One identity I-71 is associated with four fictional account representations across three sources. Stockroom source S-73 supplies two accounts with deliberately separate approved daily/admin purposes.
- Technical IDs are non-runnable teaching stand-ins. Account logins and employee number remain consistent within this independent lesson; no continuing biography from other lessons is assumed.
- Shared/non-human-account responsibility is not represented as personal correlation to all users of the account. No Machine Identity Security or Identity Graph license is assumed.
- Source data, correlation, identity attributes, account attributes, request selection, automatic selection, and target sign-in mapping are distinct mechanisms.
- Account Attribute transform default ordering and null handling are scoped only to that value lookup. Multiple Account Options do not become a general account input selector.
- Automatic profile criteria apply in the documented role/lifecycle context for multiple accounts on that source. They do not apply to Request Center request account selection or single-account identities. No/multiple-match failure is scoped to the applicable provisioning, not every operation in a wider batch.
- Source-owner form selection requires manual selection enablement and does not prove delivered access or external administrator authority.
- Main request assumes neither Stockroom account holds stock.export and the identity does not already hold the profile. Repeat-profile and payroll cases are explicitly separate variations with a prior association.
- Request, approval, supported external operation, reported outcome, target observation, and later aggregation are not collapsed into one status. No automatic immediate reaggregation or fixed delivery time is promised.
- Account-based Stockroom runtime authorization is fictional application behavior. Other application authorization and SSO/session designs require their own verification; ISC correlation does not merge credentials or runtime permissions.
- External account disablement, identity lifecycle state, session termination, wrong-person correlation, uncorrelated status, ISC representation removal, and external deletion are kept distinct. No universal disable/delete cascade is asserted.
- Full schema, transform, role, lifecycle, account-selection configuration, and connector rename mechanics remain in their primary course lessons. The current lesson supplies the supporting meanings and practical boundaries needed for independent reasoning.
- Current official docs use Identity Services / Human Fabric naming. ISC remains the requested course title; navigation breadth is not treated as a core-course licensing guarantee.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Identity/account map](../assets/FND-007_identity_account_map.txt): one person, three source contexts, four separate account representations.
- [Account-selection contexts](../assets/FND-007_account_selection_contexts.txt): automatic role/lifecycle profile criteria versus Request Center account selection, with failure/manual-selection qualification.
- Both editable files match the inline lesson blocks and use lines no longer than 50 characters for narrow-screen readability. Branches describe relationships or selection contexts, not private platform topology or a universal backend pipeline.
- Local and live publishing checks separately verify complete text, headings, tables, diagrams, and navigation. Neither check replaces a beginner read-through.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Four rows | Relationship cardinality and source/account/identity counts | One identity, four accounts, three sources; inspect identity IDs before claiming person duplication | Every account row is another identity | Sources; correlation; identity profiles |
| Right permission, wrong login | Account-specific access versus person-level presentation | Trace held permission to admin account, compare intended daily target, request/operation evidence and actual application context | Access associated with a person is usable through every correlated login | Sources; identity views; monitoring |
| Two automatic matches | Criteria scope and failure behavior | Multiple matches fail that provisioning; inspect collected usage data/design and enabled manual-selection task; don't invent an oldest-account tie-breaker | Value-lookup ordering or list order determines automatic grant selection | Multiple Account Options; Account Attribute transform |
| Request selects Admin | Request selection versus automatic profile filter | Request context uses selected account; review and inspect target rather than expecting redirection | The automatic Daily criterion corrects a wrong request choice | Request configuration; Multiple Account Options |
| Only one account | Limits of a selection filter | Criteria not applied for one source account; reassess design/remaining account and actual behavior | Multiple Account Options are a universal preventive eligibility control | Multiple Account Options |
| Removed representation | Visibility/association versus target access | External account still exists; removal can reappear on collection; inspect authorized target change and continuing assignments | Deleting a displayed ISC row deletes its external access | Sources; managing accounts |
| Payroll transfer and teach-back | Independent account tracing, repeat-profile limit and safe correction | Preserve approved support access; same-profile request is blocked; assess supported entitlement/role alternatives with authorization/target selection; verify both accounts | Multiple accounts are inherently duplicate, or a profile can be re-requested indefinitely per account | Request configuration; sources; provisioning monitoring |

Questions and worked reasoning stay in the learner file. Source references, assessment intent, and review limitations stay outside it.

## ARC-001

- Lesson: [ARC-001](../lessons/ARC-001_ISC_Tenant_and_Customer_Responsibilities.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 3.1.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 31001, Supplier Register, supplier-31001, svc-supplier-isc, supplier.view, connection/privilege conditions, times, and document-service transfer are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: operational responsibility and environment boundaries, not a contractual interpretation, universal service-level commitment, private database topology, tenant-provisioning procedure, or feature-license inventory.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [SailPoint platform/ISC product description](https://www.sailpoint.com/en-au/products/identity-security-cloud) | Vendor's current description of ISC and its multi-tenant SaaS platform. Used for hosted-service context only, not marketing outcome, exact platform naming, package availability, or a guarantee of every advertised feature. |
| [Multi-tenancy: vendor architecture explanation](https://www.sailpoint.com/blog/multi-tenancy-security-scale-innovation) | SailPoint's first-party description of logical tenant data/configuration separation and shared application/infrastructure. The article was available in indexed text and web opening; the basic HTTP main-content extractor returned navigation rather than article body. The lesson does not reproduce private partitioning, encryption, comparative-security, or zero-downtime claims. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Source configuration versus external enterprise system; connector role; VA-based versus SaaS connectivity and operation-specific support. Diagrams do not place every source behind a VA or imply arbitrary external writes. |
| [ISC connector documentation landing page](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/isc/landingpages/help/landingpages/isc_landing.html) | Customer maintenance of external API keys, credentials, and permissions. This supports integration dependency ownership, not an inference that every failure is customer-caused. A fictional source's supported write is an example assumption, not a claim that a named real connector exists for Supplier Register. |
| [Managing VAs](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/manage_va.html) | SailPoint manages VA updates; customer monitoring and documented operational action remain necessary; connectivity conditions can prevent updates; long-running work can be interrupted under update conditions. No universal uninterrupted-job guarantee, manual patch recipe, fixed update window, or right to defer updates is taught. |
| [VA system and network requirements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/requirements_va.html) | Required customer deployment/hosting/network context. Cluster counts, sizing, URLs/ports, region lists, and rollout design are deliberately reserved for primary component lessons rather than copied into this overview. |
| [User-level permissions](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/user_levels.html) and [user levels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/index.html) | Tenant administrative authority, broad Org Admin meaning, combined/elevated privilege considerations. Administrative user levels are distinct from business access roles and external integration permissions. No assumption that an ISC administrator is a target-system administrator. |
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) and [account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Sources/populations/profiles within tenant configuration; input/mapping/matching responsibility and represented person/account relationships. Manager-data question supplies a wrong input that matches a wrong manager; it does not claim a failed manager match always clears an existing manager. |
| [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) and [request configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Supporting single-source entitlement bundle and configured request review; decision versus fulfilled access. Main case uses one existing correctly associated recipient account, not a multiple-account selection algorithm or automatic account creation. |
| [Automated role assignment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/role_assignment.html) | Role criteria changes and identity processing/application of configuration. Configuration save/import is separate from applicable processing and resulting provisioning outcomes. No universal processing sequence or blanket immediate propagation claim. |
| [SPConfig](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/cli/spconfig/) | Supported import/export configuration capabilities as evidence for controlled promotion. The lesson names no specific deployment tool and promises no complete tenant cloning, automatic dependency remapping, data migration, universal object support, or restoration guarantee. |
| [Instance badges](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/customization.html#enabling-instance-badges) | Configurable environment labels for sandbox/production recognition. Badge text is not a target authorization boundary or a verification of source destinations. No claim that badges are enabled by default. |
| [System notifications](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/getting_notified_org_health.html) | Customer-configured notifications for source/VA conditions and selected recipients. Notification, monitoring response, and incident resolution are separate. No one health signal establishes end-to-end service correctness. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Source/account operation, reported results, manual work and operational evidence. The example distinguishes target authorization denial, customer prerequisite correction, supported recovery of affected work, and current target confirmation. No universal retry or automatic post-change aggregation is asserted. |

### Conditions and limits

- Tenant isolation and customer operating responsibilities are different subjects. Logical separation among ISC tenants does not prevent a customer-authorized sandbox integration from reaching a live external system.
- The responsibility table is a practical operating model. Actual subscription capabilities, documented component behavior, and applicable support arrangements govern implementation. No SLA, legal allocation, data-residency guarantee, or universal tenant count is invented.
- SailPoint-hosted platform operation does not imply customer installation/patching of core ISC application servers or direct editing of private service storage.
- Customer responsibility includes validating data, matching/mapping, access meanings, recipient/account targeting, delegated administration, external integration privileges, and operational outcomes. Work can be delegated, but business accountability requires an explicit arrangement.
- Supplier Register is fictional and uses an assumed supported VA-based write path. The source integration is not the enterprise application itself. The transfer uses a SaaS connector with no customer-deployed VA.
- The integration identity svc-supplier-isc, recipient supplier-31001, and ISC administrator are separate actors. Read authentication/permission does not confer write authorization, and tenant administration does not automatically convey external privileges.
- The timeline's customer privilege change, successful read, denied write, and absent target entitlement are supplied evidence, not actual tenant observations. Without that evidence, customer fault must not be presumed from a failure.
- Successful grant, rejected grant, sandbox misdirection, wrong manager data, and dependency-restored-with-work-pending are separately stated scenarios. No cross-scenario state is silently combined.
- Component availability, software updates, operation completion, and business correctness remain distinct. VA updates are vendor-managed; customer hosting/connectivity and documented actions remain necessary. No customer manual replacement of SailPoint-managed software is recommended.
- Configuration changes, applicable processing, provisioning, observation, and external authorization are separate mechanisms. A valid source configuration/import can still be wrong for the intended business environment.
- Source/tenant/operation identifiers, times with time zones, scope and sanitized evidence support handoffs. Do not publish credentials or assume identity logs/exports are non-sensitive.
- Detailed tenant authentication, VA/clusters, SaaS execution, IQService, network operations, API authorization, release/promotion and lifecycle design remain in their planned primary lessons.
- Current official docs use Human Fabric / Identity Services naming. ISC remains the user's requested course name. Expanded documentation navigation is not treated as evidence of core subscription entitlement.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Tenant/system responsibility map](../assets/ARC-001_tenant_and_system_boundaries.txt): hosted tenant, customer-deployed VA/network context, and external application, with integration actor and recipient distinguished.
- [Request/target permission](../assets/ARC-001_request_and_target_permission.txt): supported change reaches the target authorization boundary; allowed/rejected branches and an independent successful-read observation.
- Both text assets match the inline blocks. Maximum line widths are 55 and 57 characters. The map is conceptual, not packet routing, a private worker/queue topology, or a universal synchronous pipeline.
- Local and live checks verify complete teaching text, headings, diagrams, tables, module placement, and navigation separately from beginner validation.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Healthy page, failed write | Scope of interface/read/write evidence | Page load is not connector/target-write proof; trace the specific operation and external permission result | A working dashboard proves the entire access path is healthy | Sources; monitoring; notifications |
| Wrong manager input | Customer data/configuration versus service hosting | Validate authoritative data and mapping/matching, verify corrected result and affected work; escalate contradictory product behavior if evidenced | SaaS hosting or a platform update fixes incorrect business data automatically | Identity profiles; correlation |
| Org Admin versus integration privilege | Distinct administration, connecting actor, and recipient | Tenant authority does not substitute for scoped external write permission; use authorized application/security change | One administrator permission applies across all systems and identities | User levels; connector credential responsibility |
| Sandbox writes to production | Logical tenant boundary versus actual integration destination | Valid authorized connection can write to its configured production target; inspect destination/credential/scope before testing | A sandbox label prevents external live effects | Source configuration; instance badges |
| Managed VA update versus network | Shared component operation | Inspect customer network/hosting/health and documented vendor-managed update process; don't improvise patching or ignore connectivity | VA maintenance is wholly customer-patched or wholly independent of customer operations | VA management; requirements |
| Privilege restored, work unresolved | Prerequisite repair versus completed business outcome | Recover affected work through supported process, verify target permission and wider impact; no assumed universal retry | Correcting a dependency retroactively completes prior failed work | Monitoring provisioning |
| SaaS-connector transfer | End-to-end reasoning without VA dependency | Preserve service/customer/target boundaries; accepted token doesn't supply write privilege; validate design and scoped permission, recover/verify target access | No VA means no integration dependencies or customer operational responsibility | SaaS source definition; connector credentials; monitoring |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Reference evidence, assessment intent, and review limitations stay outside it.


## ARC-006: UI, APIs, automation, and platform capabilities

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 3.6.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026. Current access-request submission contract, API authentication/authorization/version strategy, access-profile update behavior, Workflow concepts/triggers/actions/operators/history, and request/provisioning evidence reviewed.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. ReportHub, its connector and permissions, employee 36006, object/request references and all observed outcomes are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: conceptual UI/API/automation entry points, operation-specific meaning, authority, asynchronous evidence, connector continuity and Workflow logic. No executable integration recipe, real credential, private backend topology, or universal action/approval behavior is supplied.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Submit access request](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/create-access-request-v-1/) | Current per-service submission documentation: supported request follows ISC approval processes; caller user context and request conditions matter; asynchronous queued submission and 202 response are not delivery; duplicate/overlapping requests need not return immediate submission errors. Self/on-behalf-of rules vary with configuration. Machine identity, forms and sunrise/sunset details are outside this lesson. |
| [API reference, v2025 documented bundle](https://developer.sailpoint.com/redoc/sailpoint-api-v2025-light.html) | Supplementary contract cross-check: createAccessRequest's 202 AccessRequestResponse contains tracking information for new/existing requests and access-request IDs; patchAccessProfile supports description updates. No legacy version is recommended, no schema field names are frozen in learner prose, and no executable endpoint/payload is supplied. |
| [API authentication](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/authentication/) | PAT client ID/secret obtain an access token; user-associated and outside-user contexts are not interchangeable for endpoints requiring user levels; credential selection for unattended work must follow the selected operation. A client secret is not the resource API bearer token. No recommendation to use an engineer's unrestricted personal credential for production automation. |
| [API authorization](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/authorization/) | User-level boundary and token scopes; a read-limited token does not inherit every UI permission, and broader scope does not elevate the user's level. Endpoint-specific caller/authorization requirements remain relevant. No scope-merging algorithm or universal role matrix is taught. |
| [API versioning strategy](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/api-versioning-strategy/) | Current service-specific contracts and public versus Experimental stability; check operation/version documentation rather than freezing historical version names or copying undocumented browser calls. No migration deadline or current version inventory is asserted. |
| [Workflow overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/workflows/index.html) | Workflow trigger/input/steps, actions, operators and end conditions; UI-managed definition versus execution. Workflow is not every internal ISC process or a capability/permission bypass. |
| [Workflow triggers](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/workflows/workflow-triggers.html) | Relevant identity-change event and trigger filtering; nonmatching event need not create an execution. The example supplies an enabled Workflow and available event, not a claim that every change triggers every Workflow. |
| [Workflow actions](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/workflows/workflow-actions.html) | Action-specific behavior and inputs, including notifications and integration authentication. Built-in actions are not all equivalent to a personal-token public API call. No retired access-request action name or blanket bypass/preservation of every approval for every automation action is asserted. |
| [Workflow operators](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/workflows/workflow-operators.html) | Choice/data-flow behavior and successful end; success is scoped to the configured execution rather than proving a target permission. |
| [Managing Workflows](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/workflows/workflow-manage.html) | Saved definition, enabled state, sample-input tests and real event path are distinct; execution history provides inputs, outputs, path and configuration evidence. No fixed retention period or undocumented execution ordering is asserted. |
| [Request configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) and [access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Supporting requestability/review and one-source entitlement bundle. The description-only update is deliberately narrower than edits to membership/entitlements or enabled/requestability settings. No claim that all configuration edits have no access effects. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Request/operation evidence versus target-state confirmation. Accepted submission, approval, tracked delivery and actual external permission remain separate observations. No instant refresh, guaranteed retry, rollback or timeout-equals-no-effect promise. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html), [account loading](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), [sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) and [SaaS connectivity](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/connectivity/saas-connectivity/) | Supporting definitions/path boundaries retain same-day checks recorded in earlier lessons: account/person association, collected information, source/connector roles, hosted SaaS connector execution versus VA-based work. No claim that all connectors support automatic delivery. |

### Conditions and limits

- Devi's identity I-36, profile AP-36 and request AR-36 are different non-runnable stand-ins. Employee 36006 and external username report-36006 are not interchangeable with those references. No credential value is supplied.
- ReportHub Production has one intended, existing, correctly correlated eligible account. Read quality reports contains only report.read, which grants reading under the fictional target model. Requestability, applicable checks, configured approval and supported automatic delivery without account creation/custom scripts are explicit assumptions.
- UI and API submissions are alternatives for the same intended request, not two required submissions. The comparison supplies Devi's user context and appropriate token authority; it is not a universal self-request or on-behalf-of permission promise.
- The UI can combine interactions. No one-to-one public endpoint mapping, shared internal endpoint, private service topology or undocumented browser-call contract is invented.
- A description-only supported update saves descriptive configuration. Other edits can have different effects. Reading objects, enabling requests, requesting access, aggregation and provisioning are not interchangeable operations.
- Accepted asynchronous submission is not approval or delivery. Preserve the relevant request references, inspect existing work/status, and follow the chosen contract before repeating. Ending the client is not inferred cancellation.
- The ISC API caller and external integration actor svc-report-isc are separate. A token accepted by ISC does not supply target credentials or cure target privilege failure. Changing entry point does not change the configured connector runtime.
- The notification-only Workflow is enabled and receives an available relevant event. A nonmatching trigger filter produces no execution in this example; an internal false choice can end an execution successfully without sending notification. Neither case grants access.
- Saved configuration, sample-input testing, actual event matching, execution path, action result and subsequent asynchronous outcome have different evidence scopes. No fake real-tenant execution is claimed.
- Available actions, licensing, permissions and connector operations remain conditional. The lesson does not say every Workflow action uses the same credentials or follows the access-request endpoint's approval behavior.
- UI/API comparisons require equivalent tenant, object, actor authority, operation, filters, pagination and observation time. A first page and an older account view cannot establish missing objects or failed delivery by themselves.
- Credential ownership, rotation, least privilege and failure handling are design requirements, not an executable authentication/runbook recipe. No exact token lifetime, rate limit, API page size, retention period or duplicate-protection guarantee is asserted.
- Current service documentation and supported versions govern real integrations; public does not mean anonymous. Product branding/packaging can change without making the course a license guarantee.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Entry points and delivery](../assets/ARC-006_entry_points_and_delivery.txt): alternative UI/API request entry, applicable checks, configured approval/delivery, connector work and successful target effect.
- [Workflow trigger and choice](../assets/ARC-006_workflow_trigger_and_choice.txt): trigger filter before execution versus internal notification decision after execution begins.
- Both assets match the inline blocks exactly; maximum line widths are 46 and 46 characters. Nearby text explains conditions, arrow scope, omitted implementation detail and lack of guaranteed success.
- Local/live checks separately verify complete content, diagram text, tables, placement and navigation; visual checks assess rendered readability, not learner comprehension or actual tenant outcomes.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UI works, read-only token rejects update | UI authority versus token scope | Inspect supported operation and required justified scope; do not broaden blindly or use undocumented calls | A valid token inherits all its owner's UI capabilities, or denial proves missing API capability | Authentication; authorization |
| Description-only profile update | Operation-specific success | Saved descriptive configuration is not an assignment; no account change was requested | Every successful profile save grants access | Access-profile update contract |
| Accepted request, client exits | Asynchronous tracking and decision stage | Preserve reference; supplied pending approval means submitted work remains tracked; no inferred cancellation or duplicate submission | Caller lifetime controls downstream request completion | Submission contract; request configuration |
| Successful no-notification Workflow | Definition/path versus misleading name | Inspect actual steps and false branch; successful configured end proves no grant in notification-only case | Workflow success or its name proves access delivery | Operators; execution history; supplied definition |
| First API page versus UI list | Collection scope | Follow documented pagination and compare equivalent filters/object types | One successful page is the complete population or evidence of deletion | Selected list-operation contract; supplied pagination facts |
| External training-application scheduler | Transfer of authority, tracking and outcome design | Token acquisition is not accepted request; preserve references, reconcile existing work, track decisions/delivery and define scoped unattended ownership/recovery | Automation success is merely obtaining a token and sending/repeating writes | Authentication/authorization; submission contract; provisioning evidence |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.


## ARC-007: ISC sign-in, identity providers, and source passwords

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 3.7.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026. Current official identity-profile sign-in settings, PTA, SAML SP/JIT, MFA, password policy/reset/application/sync behavior and session/lockout boundaries reviewed.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 37007, AccessGate, CaseDesk, their records, integration actor, reported errors and all successful/failed outcomes are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: authentication and credential-change execution boundaries, not a SAML implementation/security runbook, advanced password-management deployment, IdP product tutorial, authenticator-reset procedure or authority to change real credentials.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative-source identity establishment versus profile configuration; site username/password and Directory Connection meanings; MFA and reset/unlock settings. HR is the fictional authoritative source, not automatically the authentication system. No complete identity-profile or lifecycle mechanism is inferred. |
| [Configuring PTA](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/pta.html) | Supported authentication-source credential checks, profile-level Directory Connection, prior account aggregation, account-selection dependencies, source-password linkage and risk of changing production authentication sources without support. Main alternate case supplies one source account; no provisioning selector is substituted for documented PTA selection. |
| [ISC as a service provider](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/config_isc_service_provider.html) | External IdP/ISC SP relationship, password not shared with ISC through SAML federation, matching-user requirement, participant/login/mapping/signing-certificate configuration, failed-authentication path, controlled elevated-user direct sign-in and lack of SAML Single Logout. No universal employee bypass, fixed session lifetime or private validation sequence is taught. |
| [JIT account creation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/jit-accounts.html) | Separately configured JIT source/profile/SP account-and-identity creation; not implicit in SSO and not JIT access activation or universal business-app account creation. Main example explicitly excludes this option. Services guidance and full configuration mechanics remain out of scope. |
| [MFA configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/strong_auth.html) | ISC authenticator and reset context versus IdP sign-in, elevated-user registration/direct-sign-in conditions, TOTP and distinction from password-update verification. Learner prose does not assert that every IdP sign-in prompts for an additional ISC code or that SSO removes all registration responsibilities. |
| [Password Management overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/pwd/index.html) and [Password Manager user help](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/user-help/password_manager.html) | Supported configured source-password management and user-facing applications/groups versus site-password management. Standalone, shared-source and sync-group presentations are different relationships; no feature/license or universal connector guarantee. |
| [Access applications for passwords](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/pwd/add_access_app.html) | Required application/source configuration, user visibility, passwords managed per source, multiple applications sharing source password, PTA linkage and sync-group effects. Application tiles are not independent password stores or SSO trusts. No claim that merely creating an application enables every operation. |
| [Password policies](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/pwd/pwd_policies/pwd_policies.html) | Compatible source/ISC policy requirements; configured verification enforcement; support limited to applicable direct connections, not flat files. Does not recommend disabling verification, choosing a universal policy value or copying target restrictions without checking the actual connector/system. |
| [Reset and unlock authentication](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/pwd/pwd_reset.html) | Profile-configured recovery methods versus policy enforcement; verification needs usable configured information; disabled-account reset restriction. No exact method order, phone-country list, code lifetime or unsupported recovery shortcut is taught. |
| [Admin password reset](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/reset_pwd_auth.html) | Sending reset instructions versus user completion and PTA/sync interactions. No actual email, MFA reset, identity deregistration or password change was performed. |
| [Password sync groups](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/pwd/sync_grps.html) | Supported multi-source propagation and policy compatibility; normal SSO precedence over Directory Connection; source-based history validation; PTA failure gating versus non-PTA partial outcomes; source failures visible in audit evidence. No universal transaction/rollback, exact retry schedule, readable password history or reverse synchronization from every external website is asserted. |
| [Session settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/session_lengths.html) | ISC maximum/idle session controls and browser session-cookie retention caveat. Exact limits/defaults are omitted; closing a browser or changing a password is not generalized to all external session/token termination. |
| [Lockout settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/lockout.html) | Supporting sign-in failure/lockout distinctions and interaction with authentication/recovery settings. No lockout threshold or advice to repeat guesses is supplied. |
| [Sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html), [account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html), [loading accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), [user levels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/user_levels.html) and [provisioning evidence](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Supporting concepts retain same-day verification in earlier lessons. Correlation is ownership association, not sign-in mapping or shared credentials; collection is not live password proof; sign-in does not grant administration; integration result must be scoped to the intended account/operation. |

### Conditions and limits

- Devi is independently established as a support-operations employee, number 37007. Workforce HR account hr-37007, directory account corp-37007, AccessGate user devi37007, ISC username devi37007 and CaseDesk account cases-37007 are distinct records even where labels coincide.
- Main setup supplies existing correctly associated accounts, configured trusted SAML sign-in through AccessGate and a matching ISC user. JIT creation is disabled. CaseDesk uses its own password and is outside shared-password/sync relationships.
- Fictional email devi37007@corp.example is the intended mapped identity value; old.example is a supplied mismatch. No real assertion, password, signing key, certificate or recovery code is included.
- SAML trust acceptance and user mapping are different checks; the diagram is not a complete cryptographic validation algorithm or private backend topology. Passwords are not copied to managed apps by this sign-in relationship.
- The PTA example is an alternate route, not a second step required after external SSO. Corporate Directory is assumed supported/configured and has one relevant pre-aggregated account; no universal account-choice algorithm or connector protocol is invented.
- Authentication dependencies vary by supported source/runtime. A VA is introduced only conditionally; it is not inserted into native or every federated/SaaS sign-in path.
- Native site credentials, source credentials, IdP authority, ISC user level and application permissions are separate. A shared user label or HR identity does not create credential synchronization or admin authority.
- MFA at the IdP, ISC authenticator settings and verification for password changes have distinct configuration/ownership. Reset, unlock, reenable and MFA reset are not interchangeable operations or permission to bypass security.
- CaseDesk is a hypothetical password-capable direct connection with configured policy/access application/recovery and intended eligible account. svc-case-isc is the target integration actor, not the recipient or browser user. There is no claim that a real CaseDesk connector exists.
- Password validation is conceptual: required verification and applicable policy/target checks matter, but exact internal validation order, account-selection behavior and synchronous response timing are not prescribed.
- Password Manager is not a readable secret inventory. Imported account data and flat-file collection do not enable external password operations.
- Same-source application groupings and multi-source password sync groups are distinct. PTA source-change gating is not generalized to all arrangements; the separate non-PTA exercise explicitly supplies mixed outcomes.
- Relevant audit/target/authentication evidence is needed for the particular operation. An emailed link, successful verification, old account view or existing application session is not proof of completed external password delivery.
- No guaranteed retries, atomic group transaction, rollback, reverse synchronization, password history implementation or immediate session/token revocation is asserted.
- ISC's lack of SAML SLO is not a claim about every IdP or application's logout implementation. Session settings and browser retention remain service-specific. Emergency access is approved, controlled elevated-user access, not a universal employee bypass.
- Official documentation currently uses Identity Services / Human Fabric branding. ISC is retained as the requested course name; feature availability and packaging must still be verified for a real tenant.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Federated sign-in](../assets/ARC-007_federated_sign_in.txt): browser/IdP response, ISC trust/mapping acceptance and resulting ISC session; not target-password delivery.
- [Directory sign-in](../assets/ARC-007_directory_sign_in.txt): supplied source credentials checked through the configured integration, source result and remaining applicable checks; not SAML federation.
- [Source password change](../assets/ARC-007_source_password_change.txt): required verification/policy, supported connector operation, target change and account-specific result inspection. The final inspection is human verification, not a guaranteed automatic fresh login.
- Assets match inline blocks exactly; maximum line widths are 39, 44 and 43 characters. Captions explain successful-example conditions, omitted processing detail and failure scope.
- Local and live publication checks separately verify full text, headings, tables, diagrams, placement and navigation. Rendered readability is not observed learner comprehension or tenant execution.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Successful IdP, mismatched email | Authentication versus ISC identity mapping | Compare supplied values and approved mapping; correct responsible data/configuration without duplicate identity or credential reset | IdP success always means ISC success, or matching errors mean wrong password | SAML SP settings; JIT conditions |
| PTA source unreachable | Dependency versus rejected proof | Investigate actual configured source/integration; no accepted/rejected credential decision is supplied | Every login failure proves wrong password or justifies switching authentication source | PTA; source connection dependencies |
| Missing Admin menu after sign-in | Authentication versus platform authorization | Inspect intended user level; wider authority requires approved justification | Certificate repair or successful login should grant administration | User levels; SAML authentication |
| Standalone CaseDesk reset | Operation/account scope | Only supplied standalone target changes; correlation/user naming creates no sync | One credential reset changes all accounts | Password Manager; access applications |
| Non-PTA sync group mixed results | Per-source outcome and policy compatibility | Reconcile changed versus rejected targets, correct actual restriction and recover through supported process | Group work is universally all-or-nothing or first success proves all succeeded | Sync groups; password policies |
| Verification succeeds, connector denied | Recovery proof versus target-operation authority | Inspect integration actor's required justified privilege and verify target outcome; do not reset unrelated authenticator | User verification supplies the connector's password-change authority | Password-management source support; fictional denial evidence |
| Existing session after confirmed change | Credential result versus session state | Use approved fresh authentication/session checks; no failed-reset or containment conclusion follows from old tab | Password change immediately ends every session, or continuing session proves reset failure | Session settings; lack of SAML SLO; supplied application facts |
| Purchasing application proposal | Transfer across identity data, sign-in trust, password management and permissions | Establish actual authentication, account mapping, supported password configuration/authority and distinct access design; SSO needs its own supported integration | Aggregation reveals passwords, ISC SSO federates every managed app, or all users can reset every collected account | Aggregation/correlation; SAML SP; password application/policy/sync documentation |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.


## ARC-008: Requesting, executing, and observing work

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 3.8.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026. Current request submission/status/tracking, provisioning/account-activity/manual fulfillment, collection scope/indexing and service-desk mapping reviewed; idempotency terminology checked against RFC 9110.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 38008, StockRoom, its SaaS connector, target entitlements, integration actor, record labels, UTC times and all observed results are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: architectural and evidential completion boundaries, not a full access-request configuration, provisioning engine implementation, private queue model, retry runbook, incident remediation authority or universal status-enum mapping.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Submit access request](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/create-access-request-v-1/) | Current documented 202 submission, asynchronous queued processing, applicable ISC approvals, duplicate/overlap behavior and existing-work checks. No executable payload, frozen legacy version, user-context bypass or guarantee that every client error means no submission. |
| [Access request status](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/list-access-request-status-v-1/) | Supported status inspection, caller/population restrictions, and ignored already-held requests not appearing as ordinary new status entries. Query completeness and selected contract matter. No claim that every absence proves no work or that every canceled request is hidden. |
| [Tracking requests](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/user-help/requests/tracking_access.html) | Request reference, review-step/error details and pending-stage cancellation; request status is not target verification. No universal cancel-at-any-stage, cancel-as-rollback or precise UI-navigation runbook is taught. |
| [Request configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Required requestability/approval settings and independent requested-item provisioning/cancellation. Main access profile has explicit configured checks/review; no claim that approval grants target integration privileges or every request follows identical stages. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Account/access changes, supported automatic/manual arrangements, operation configuration and documented recognized retry errors. Exact retry intervals/counts are intentionally not copied as a universal operation guarantee. Existing account and no creation dependency are supplied assumptions. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Manual task completion assertion; provisioning-event details; Account Activity action/requester/recipient/source/status/stage/modified fields and errors. Component evidence must be scoped; no universal record/cardinality, every-entitlement network call or live target measurement is inferred. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Read/collection versus external change, aggregation history and scope, Search update timing, supported single-account refresh without identity-attribute synchronization or provisioning effects. No requirement for a full aggregation after each write, exact indexing SLA, deletion inference from incomplete data, or production reprocessing command is taught. |
| [ServiceNow service desk integration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/servicenow/sdim/help/integrating_servicenow_sdim/intro.html) | Concrete documented example of ISC provisioning actions converted to service-management tickets; additional availability/licensing/configuration conditions remain applicable. Learner prose names the supported pattern rather than promising this integration in every tenant. |
| [Generic service desk completion mapping](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/generic_sd/help/integrating_generic_service_desk/best_practices.html) | Target ticket status mapping can determine when the related ISC provisioning task is treated as complete. Mapped completion is not independent target-account verification, and no specific ticket-state vocabulary or mapping is prescribed. |
| [RFC 9110, section 9.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-9.2.2) | Supporting idempotency definition: repeated equivalent intended effect rather than identical responses/no logging or universal safe retry. Applied as a reasoning distinction, not a claim that every ISC POST/connector action is idempotent. |
| [Sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html), [SaaS runtime](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/connectivity/saas-connectivity/), [VAs](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/index.html), [access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) and [correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Supporting definitions retain same-day checks recorded in preceding lessons. Hypothetical hosted StockRoom connector, one-source bundle, account association and target authority are supplied conditions. IQService is conditional supported Windows-side work, as verified in ARC-004, not an assumed part of this SaaS path. |

### Conditions and limits

- Devi is independently established as a warehouse stock planner, employee 38008. Her existing stock-38008 account is correctly associated and eligible for the main request. No account creation, future date, custom script or manual fulfillment dependency is assumed in that case.
- StockRoom Production is the ISC source label; the intended external production destination and account must also match. svc-stock-isc is the external integration actor, not the recipient, reviewer or API caller.
- Stock planning access contains stock.read and stock.export from this source. The fictional target model requires both for the full planning requirement. Neither is initially present.
- AP-38, AR-38 and ACT-38 are separate non-runnable stand-ins. Their suffix isn't an ISC identifier-derivation rule or a guarantee of one-to-one record relationships.
- The main trace supplies separate permission effects: read added, export refused for target authority. No guarantee of one call/activity per entitlement, universal ordering, atomic execution or rollback is asserted.
- All times use one fictional UTC day: 09:00 collected baseline, 10:00 acceptance, 10:05 approval, 10:06 mixed delivery, 10:08 old view, 10:20 inclusive collection, 11:00 prerequisite repair, 11:10 supported recovery, 11:12 target confirmation and 11:15 business test. These are not measured performance, scheduled intervals or a product SLA.
- The older account view is a supplied observation. The later collection explicitly includes the intended account and relevant memberships. Neither observation implies that every ISC account update requires source-wide aggregation or that aggregation grants access.
- Successful single-account refresh doesn't establish identity-attribute recalculation. Browser refresh is a view interaction, not automatic aggregation, reprocessing or provisioning.
- Request status, review decision, account activity, component effect, collection outcome and actual target state retain separate scope. A historical failure can coexist with successful later recovery without rewriting the earlier event.
- Missing results require checking caller/view/tenant/filter/time/history/pagination context. The on-behalf-of exercise supplies another authorized requester, so filtering submissions by Devi is not equivalent to filtering requests for Devi.
- Timeout and lost initiating responses leave uncertainty until evidence resolves it. No universal no-effect, duplicate protection, rollback, automatic retry or exactly-once result is promised.
- Recovery requires current authorization, actual-state reconciliation and operation-specific supported procedure. Repairing privileges alone isn't execution. Canceling pending work isn't evidence that applied target changes were undone.
- Manual/ticket fulfillment cases are alternative arrangements, not extra stages for the automatic SaaS example. Completed task/ticket assertions need appropriate target evidence under the agreed process.
- Authorized target inspection and business testing are fictional supplied evidence, not actions performed by the author. No real request, access change, source repair, aggregation, ticket creation, client execution or credential handling occurred.
- Current official documentation uses Identity Services / Human Fabric naming. ISC remains the requested course title. Licensing, feature visibility, connector capability and supported versions must still be checked for a real project.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Request to verification](../assets/ARC-008_request_to_verification.txt): request/check/review/delivery/target relationship ending in explicitly human outcome inspection.
- [Partial target outcome](../assets/ARC-008_partial_target_outcome.txt): two required permission effects and supplied partial result, not simultaneous calls or separate approvals.
- [Change versus collection](../assets/ARC-008_change_and_collection.txt): outbound supported change versus inbound later read, not a guaranteed cycle.
- All three assets match inline blocks exactly. Maximum line widths are 38, 43 and 41 characters. Captions identify assumptions, actor/direction, omitted internals and non-guaranteed success.
- Local and live checks separately verify complete text, headings, tables, diagrams, placement and navigation. Rendered readability is not beginner comprehension or observed tenant execution.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Approved, work in progress | Decision versus execution outcome | Follow relevant activity/dependencies; supplied evidence proves neither completion nor target rejection | Approval is delivery, or delay proves failure and justifies repeat | Submission/tracking; request configuration |
| Read delivered, export refused | Partial outcome and target evidence | Preserve successful read, investigate missing export authority and recover supported unresolved work | Overall error means no effect or universal rollback | Provisioning evidence; supplied target facts |
| Successful collection, export absent | Observation versus mutation | Collection correctly represented missing permission; delivery remains unresolved | Aggregation grants or repairs requested access | Loading accounts; provisioning |
| Timeout, target confirms change | Recorded response versus actual effect | Reconcile the successful target change through supported process without blindly repeating | Timeout proves no effect | Supplied target/audit facts; scoped monitoring; RFC retry distinction |
| Manual completion on test account | Completion assertion versus correct target | Confirm intended production source/account/permission and preserve mismatch | Task badge or any same-name account proves fulfillment | Manual task tracking; source/account context |
| First status page filtered by recipient-as-requester | Query semantics and incomplete evidence | On-behalf requester differs; inspect known references, correct supported population, caller rights and pagination | Missing first-page entry means never submitted | Status API contract; supplied query context |
| Payroll completion design | Transfer of scope, asynchronous stages and recovery | Define decision/execution/target evidence, actual fulfillment arrangement, reference tracking and partial/uncertain recovery; reads aren't grants | Submission success is business completion and repeat-plus-aggregation repairs every failure | Submission/status/tracking; provisioning/manual/service desk; account loading |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.


## SRC-001: Source purpose and represented population

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.1.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026. Current source definition/configuration/connection/UI/ownership, account-schema, identity-profile and connector-specific filtering references reviewed. Supporting collection/correlation/provisioning checks retain the same-day verification in earlier lessons.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 41001, DispatchHub, its SaaS connector, source/account labels, region/classification logic, inventory and all outcomes are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: source purpose, external context, coverage agreement, evidence and operational boundaries. Detailed authoritative/profile-priority mechanics, capability matrices, schemas, filter syntax, aggregation modes and deletion rules remain their own planned primary lessons.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
