| [Configuring sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/config_sources.html) | Source definition versus external application; connector-specific connection/authentication settings; unique label/description, assigned owner, direct/file choices and source capability configuration. Current documentation distinguishes read-only and supported deep-governance modes. The lesson does not recommend enabling provisioning or imply that generic VA setup text overrides documented SaaS paths. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Source/connector/connection distinctions; actual connectivity details vary; SaaS runtime can avoid a customer VA cluster; collected source account UI shows associations and multiple accounts, with Correlated/Uncorrelated view filters. Reset/delete operations affect data and dependencies and are not treated as incident diagnosis. |
| [Source schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Attribute/schema and designated account-identifier meanings; actual account IDs must distinguish accounts, even when a person reference is shared; changing identifier definitions after aggregation is unsafe without appropriate design. No full schema recipe, fixed field list, or universal username-as-native-ID rule is taught. |
| [PingOne SaaS filters](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/pingone/help/saas_connectivity/pingone/configuring_filters.html) | Concrete primary-source evidence that supported filtering differs by object, field and operator. Used only to verify connector-specific filtering exists; its syntax, population endpoints and attribute matrix are not generalized to DispatchHub or every ISC connector. Population in learner prose is a project account set, not a required ISC object. |
| [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative source establishes identities through its profile; identity settings/mappings and source responsibilities differ from additional application-account collection. The DispatchHub case is explicitly non-authoritative and uses an existing personnel-established identity. No blanket trusted-source ranking or profile-priority algorithm is introduced here. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Supporting account-to-identity association using suitable source/identity values; missing collected record versus missing ownership association. The same-day check in prior lessons is retained. An employee-number comparison is an explicit fictional supported matching configuration, not an instruction to use that value as the unique external account key. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Supporting account collection, source/view/run scope, pagination/completeness reasoning, represented state and conditional handling of missing records. Detailed account-deletion safeguards and aggregation mechanics are not replaced with a universal rule. |
| [Provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) and [SaaS connectivity](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/connectivity/saas-connectivity/) | Supported external changes differ from collecting data; hypothetical hosted connector and target authority do not imply every read filter constrains every write. Current enabled capabilities and actual destination/operation privileges must be checked separately. |
| [Access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) and [request configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Supporting one-source entitlement bundle and separately configured requestability/review/delivery. A collected account or permission and an application/source label are not a complete access-control configuration. |

### Conditions and limits

- Devi is independently established as a West warehouse coordinator, employee 41001, with existing external standard depot-41001 and approved admin depot-admin-41001 accounts. Both are intended to be represented and linked to her existing ISC identity; neither is initially read by the East-only production collection.
- Dispatch-Prod and Dispatch-Test are separate external organizations. S-41P and S-41T are non-runnable source-ID stand-ins. The diagram illustrates this project's choice of separate configurations, not a universal one-source-per-application/environment/partition rule.
- The real source label is DispatchHub Production, but labels do not prove destination. A duplicate sandbox account string is not production identity evidence.
- Inventory is stable for the supplied comparison: 100 standard human accounts, ten additional admin accounts belonging to ten of those same people, and ten integration service accounts. Total 120; agreed source coverage 110 human-operated accounts across 100 people.
- The human set includes disabled accounts and both regions. Its supplied split is 70 East plus 40 West, including Devi's two accounts. Equal counts do not establish equal membership or correctness of access data.
- The ten service accounts are deliberately outside this particular source and require separately accountable treatment. This is not a general recommendation to exclude non-human accounts or regard them as low-risk/ungoverned.
- svc-dispatch-isc can read the full intended production set in the main incident. A saved supported region condition restricts selection to East; the complete selected pages are returned without processing failures. Thus filter cause is supplied evidence, not guessed from 70 alone.
- The fictional connector supports the stated human-account selection and region condition. No actual DispatchHub connector offering, universal field/filter syntax, protocol, runtime implementation or hidden pipeline is asserted.
- Corrected supported read preserves destination, credential context, account identifiers and matching configuration, then returns the agreed 110 accounts. Supplied matching values associate Devi's two records with her existing identity; no universal matching guarantee for every returned account follows.
- Account identifiers are distinct in this external context, while employee number is a shared person reference. The schema example does not prescribe changing a live identifier or use employee number as a unique account key.
- Uncorrelated means an acquired account has not been matched to the intended person identity; uncollected means the expected account was not acquired. The operational orphaned-account explanation concerns missing established responsibility, not a promise about a specific product flag or automatic deletion.
- UI filters and pagination can hide represented accounts independently of read configuration. A valid connection, successful selected-set aggregation, visible correlated subset or expected row count is not full coverage proof.
- Account-row coverage and access-information coverage are separate. Supported attributes, classification, membership information, observation time and expected-set comparison remain relevant.
- Main onboarding is read-only with a hypothetical SaaS connector. Read scope is not asserted to be an enforcement boundary for all writes; a broad external credential is not project authorization to use its write permissions.
- Authoritative/profile primer supplies enough local meaning but does not replace later source-role/priority mechanisms. Collected permissions are not automatically requestable, delivered or certified.
- Scope corrections do not prove target deprovisioning. Handling of previously collected missing data depends on operation/configuration/safeguards. No automatic delete, always-retain, rollback or harmless reset assumption is made.
- Source owner, integration operator, external administrator and account owner remain distinct responsibilities. A source assignment does not itself grant target privileges or settle all data-accountability questions.
- Current documentation uses Identity Services / Human Fabric branding. ISC remains the course name. Actual connector features, licensing, permissions and supported configuration must be checked for a real project.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Production and sandbox](../assets/SRC-001_production_and_sandbox.txt): external organization to its chosen source context and represented records.
- [Intended and collected population](../assets/SRC-001_intended_and_collected_population.txt): required 110-account human set split into selected 70 East and wrongly excluded 40 West accounts.
- Assets match inline blocks exactly. Maximum line widths are 40 and 46 characters. Captions state direction, assumed boundary and omitted implementation/operation detail.
- Local/live publication checks separately verify full text, headings, tables, diagrams, new Module 4 placement and navigation. Rendered readability is not observed beginner understanding or tenant execution.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Working East-only source | Connection/run success versus agreed coverage | Saved supported condition and complete selected-set results establish West omission; correct approved read scope and verify later inclusive data | Successful test or aggregation equals full project coverage; missing account requires creation or correlation changes | Source configuration; connector-specific filters; supplied complete 70-set evidence |
| Production label, test destination | Label versus external context | Compare actual organization/returned records with intended production set; renaming alone doesn't change destination | Familiar source/account name proves correct environment | Source connectivity context; supplied organizations |
| 110 accounts for 100 people | Account and person cardinality | Preserve distinct legitimate standard/admin records and owner relationships; no forced equal totals | Every account needs a separate person, or unequal counts prove duplicate accounts to delete | Source account UI/multiple accounts; schemas; supplied inventory |
| Collected account missing employee number | Read versus ownership association | Inspect missing matching value/configuration and supported correction; broader read doesn't invent ownership data | Every absent identity linkage is a filter issue | Correlation; schema fields; supplied record |
| Read filter and broad write credential | Operation-specific authority | Verify supported write scope/target authority and approved least privilege; collection filter isn't undocumented write security | Selected read population enforces every external operation boundary | Source/connector capability distinctions; supplied credential facts |
| Correct total, incorrect members | Set membership versus count | Preserve missing and unexpected members, investigate classification/selection and verify correct set | Equal totals prove coverage or out-of-scope service accounts can be ignored | Coverage agreement; supplied set comparison |
| Monthly active payroll export proposal | Transfer of external context, source role, data and removal scope | Establish actual account inventory versus employees, identifiers/owners/status/export/time, authoritative role and separate supported authorized removal evidence | Successful employee-file import means full account coverage and absent rows justify deletion | Source/file/schema/profile definitions; conditional account-deletion/provisioning behavior |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## SRC-002: Authoritative and non-authoritative source responsibilities

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.2.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026. Identity-profile selection, creation-order defaults, mapped inputs, required attributes and exceptions, profile deletion, correlation, identity processing, PTA and authoritative-account removal were freshly checked against official ISC documentation.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 42002, all four accounts, I-42, profile priorities, configured mappings, the corrected association and resulting values are supplied fictional facts.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is a separate check.
- Scope: source responsibility and the causal distinction between identity origin, account ownership, applicable profile, attribute inputs, authentication and external changes. Detailed profile implementation, manager correlation, transforms, aggregation/deletion settings, lifecycle design and provisioning remain their own primary lessons.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Primary authority for one profile per identity; highest-priority applicable profile among authoritative sources holding its accounts; lower numerical values mean higher precedence; creation-order default; mappings can select other source/account attributes and supported calculation logic. Required uid/email/lastname and unique uid across profiles; email non-null check does not validate email format. Mapping preview, exceptions, Needs Processing and supported application of changes distinguish configuration from observed outcomes. Profile deletion does not delete the source or external accounts, can make the source non-authoritative and remove identities unless reassignment is possible, and can leave accounts uncorrelated. No deletion recipe or fixed processing latency is taught. |
| [Configuring sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/config_sources.html) | Authoritative source designation requires the profile/mapping arrangement to complete identity-onboarding configuration. Source capability and read-only/provisioning configuration are separate from authority. No real setting is changed. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Configured matching associates source accounts with existing identities; unmatched records can remain uncorrelated. The four example associations are supplied facts, not a claim that equal names or employee references automatically merge records. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Collected account relationships and uncorrelated-account shadow identity representation. A shadow placeholder is distinguished from successful authoritative personnel onboarding; no one-shadow-per-account count or private storage model is invented. |
| [Managing accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html) | Supported account reassignment and duplicate-identity correction have their own prerequisites and consequences. Profile priority is not presented as an identity-merging operation or a correction recipe. |
| [Identity processing](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Processing evaluates mapped attributes and relevant lifecycle/role/access behavior under documented event/scheduled/manual paths. Starting provisioning does not validate its successful completion. No universal schedule, internal queue, retry order or atomic migration is claimed. |
| [Configuring pass-through authentication](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/pta.html) | Directory Connection/PTA selects a supported credential-check source and uses relevant aggregated accounts. It need not be the authoritative identity-origin source. The fictional example assumes a supported configured connection and one intended directory account, not automatic SSO or credential fallback to HR. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Authoritative-account removal under supported full-aggregation/account-deletion conditions can lead to reassignment and recalculation when another authoritative account remains; otherwise deletion/hidden handling has conditions and blockers. Status-value changes and temporary outages are not account removal or guaranteed immediate deprovisioning. |
| [Manager correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/manager_correlation.html) | Manager relationships use separately documented matching and profile/source evaluation behavior. The department/email example is deliberately not generalized into a universal rule for manager relationships. Full manager logic is deferred to its dedicated lesson. |
| [Provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) and [tracking provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Supporting definitions and evidence boundaries: authoritative designation is not connector operation support or target authorization; identity-side calculated values and initiated work do not prove external delivery. |
| [Access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Supporting access-profile meaning: a bundle of entitlements from one source, distinct from the identity profile controlling identity attributes/settings. Detailed assignment mechanics are not introduced as part of source authority. |

### Diagram and example checks

The two inline text diagrams match their editable assets exactly after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 43 and 45 characters. The first represents identity origin versus account association, not execution order; the second follows two configured attribute inputs, not a cross-source priority algorithm.

Employee number 42002 is a shared person reference, while emp-42002, ctr-42002, corp-42002 and expense-42002 are source-specific account identifiers. I-42 is a fictional non-runnable identity-ID stand-in. Both authoritative accounts already belong to I-42. Initial Contractors 10 / Employees 20 selects Contractors; approved Employees 10 / Contractors 20 selects Employees after appropriate processing in the supplied result. Department moves from Vendor Services to Finance; the correct directory-sourced email remains devi42002@corp.example. This is not a universal population-migration guarantee or tenant test.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| HR Finance but Contractors applies | Every authoritative source overrides the identity, or last aggregation wins | Existing same-identity account associations, priority and the applicable department mapping explain Vendor Services; inspect approved order and processing, not rewrite correct HR data. Primary profile and processing references above. |
| Non-authoritative directory email | Every identity attribute must come from the authoritative source | Supported profile mappings can use the correct directory account without changing its identity-origin role. Primary profile reference above. |
| Required email missing | Collection success or a lower-priority address implies automatic fallback | Inspect the selected mapping, input and explicitly configured alternatives; resolve the reported required-value exception. No invented automatic per-field cross-profile fallback. |
| Separate identities | Profile priority merges person records | Priority operates on authoritative accounts already associated with an identity; investigate matching/associations and supported correction separately. Profile and account-management references above. |
| PTA directory unavailable | HR authority provides alternative credential checking | Check the separately configured authentication dependency and reported result; identity establishment does not bypass it. PTA reference above. |
| Read-only connector made authoritative | Authority upgrades operation support | Establish supported provisioning/configuration/target privileges; source identity origin doesn't grant writes and can affect unintended populations. Source/provisioning references above. |
| Employment status changes, account remains | Status changes or outages equal authoritative-account deletion | Check the actual relationship, lifecycle/processing behavior and profile association; no supplied removal means no demonstrated fallback or completed access removal. Profile/loading references above. |
| Acquisition-design transfer | Aggregation order can define populations, identity ownership and every value | Establish population/overlap/account associations, applicable priority, attribute inputs and required values, explicit fallback where needed, authentication and write paths, change impact and separate identity/target verification. Combines the named documented responsibilities without inventing a migration recipe. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

| [Web Services SaaS supported features](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/supported_features.html) | Selected connector family offers account/entitlement reads, Get Object, delta and provisioning operation types including create/update, entitlement add/remove, enable/disable, delete, unlock and password change. These framework capabilities are not claims about the fictional FulfillHub API or automatic configuration. |
| [Web Services SaaS Add Operations](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/add_operations.html) and [General Information](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/general_information.html) | Operation-specific target/destination/method/input and response configuration is required; selecting a supported operation type isn't implementation of an unavailable target API. | [Web Services SaaS Test Connection](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/test_connection.html) | Test Connection has its own operation configuration and expected result. Its success establishes the exercised test, not a verification of every proposed create/update/remove/disable/delete behavior. |
| [Active Directory IQService prerequisite](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/iqservice_as_a_prerequisite.html) and [AD prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/prerequisites.html) | Product feature support does not provide required components, secure connectivity or target privileges. IQService requirements are identified for AD provisioning/password work; the lesson doesn't add IQService to Web Services SaaS or repeat installation/permission instructions. |
| [Configuring sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/config_sources.html) | Read-only versus supported deep-governance setup; Quick Compliance read-only restriction; enabling the documented deep-governance conversion cannot be undone. No source mode is changed and it isn't described as a harmless troubleshooting toggle. Source authority is separately tied to identity profiles, not a write upgrade. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Source creation inputs, supported access-triggered account creation, configuration features versus actual behavior, direct versus disconnected/manual fulfillment. A missing location is a conditional input example, not a universal required ISC account field. No universal retry, rollback or automatic failure-to-task conversion is asserted. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Account Activity/provisioning details and manual tasks have different evidentiary scopes. A recorded decision or completed task is not independent measurement of target membership; target and later collection must be interpreted in context. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html), [account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) and [understanding certifications](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/certs/understanding_certifications.html) | Supporting same-day checks retained from earlier authored lessons: selected runtime/source representation, configured account ownership association and reviewer decisions. These don't fill target API gaps or prove completed external changes. |

### Diagram and example checks

The two inline text diagrams match their editable assets after trailing-whitespace normalization, with maximum line widths 41 and 39 characters. The readiness diagram is a human dependency review, not undocumented backend order. The change/collection diagram separates coordinated create/add effects from later observation; it doesn't imply an all-or-nothing transaction or automatic reversal.

Employee number 43003 belongs to this independent purchasing example. fulfill-43003 is the target account; svc-fulfill-isc is the integration actor; buyers is an already-existing target group and Buyer its teaching label. The eight-row matrix separates framework support from fictional project evidence. Reads/create/department update/add have supplied positive evidence. Remove lacks a supported target API; disable has an explicit target 403 permission message and an enabled account; delete is unconfigured and untested. No real operation, source mode, endpoint, permission or fulfillment configuration was changed.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Existing groups appear in ISC | Reading groups or assigning membership implies group creation | Verify the operation on the intended group object; read/account-membership support doesn't establish group creation. Operation/object matrix references above. |
| Accounts created but disable denied | One write's success proves all permissions, or a denied action proves unsupported | Distinguish documented capability and configured operation from the specific target authority failure; the explicit message and unchanged account support the supplied conclusion, not any bare 403 globally. |
| Removal operation but no target API | Selecting an operation type invents target behavior or automatic manual fallback | Establish a supported target path or explicit approved alternative; otherwise the requirement remains open. Web Services configuration and fulfillment references above. |
| File reports disabled | Represented status means the import performed a target disable | Inspect target state and actual change activity separately; file data is an observation/assertion. Delimited File references above. |
| Department updated | Update Account proves every field can be changed | Check field-specific target/connector constraints, input, authority and identifier-change impact. No untested identifier rename is declared supported. |
| Supported but untested enable | Documented capability alone means ready, or no test means unsupported | Keep product support separate from configuration and verification; identify missing controlled evidence. Feature/configuration references above. |
| Reviewer revoked but Buyer remains | Approval/revoke decision equals completed external removal | Follow the designed fulfillment route and owner, reconcile target membership and delivery evidence; don't infer deletion or session termination. Certification and tracking references above. |
| Payroll-proposal transfer | Account load and connection test prove every proposed write | Split requirements by object/operation/field; check correct ISC connector and conditions, target paths, components, configuration, input and authority, then appropriate outcome evidence. Salary/deletion/password requirements need explicit scope and responsible-system checks; missing implementation stays unresolved or receives an approved alternative. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## SRC-004: Account schemas, identifiers and display attributes

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.4.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Official account-schema definitions/designations, discovery, subsequent collection, audit events and post-aggregation change warnings; Delimited File schema/account attributes; source-schema model properties; correlation and identity-profile mappings were freshly checked.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 44004, InvoiceTrack, both account records, integration contract, I-44, office mapping defect/correction and every outcome are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: account-schema meaning, field/value relationships, designated ID/Name, ownership matching and safe changes. Complete type/format rules, entitlement-schema linking, aggregation modes, correlation criteria/order and provisioning remain their primary lessons. No API request, schema edit or migration is performed.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Primary account-schema definition and designated Account ID/Account Name attributes; support-dependent discovery/manual definition, subsequent aggregation following saved schema, and schema-change audit events. The lesson preserves the explicit warning that Account Name is immutable and post-aggregation designation changes can create duplicate accounts/identities, while Account ID changes can break references. It does not call displayAttribute purely cosmetic or promise identifier changes are harmless. No fixed missing-key rejection, duplicate-winner, automatic reprocessing or universal rename algorithm is invented. |
| [Schema model](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/sources/models/schema/) | Technical property meanings: schema id identifies the schema; identityAttribute and displayAttribute select named attributes for object identification/display. The selected two-property JSON fragment is explanatory, not a complete request, endpoint tutorial, identity-profile mapping or account-correlation configuration. Native identity/UUID equivalence and internal storage algorithms aren't asserted. |
| [Delimited File account attributes](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/delimited_file/help/integrating_delimited_file/account_attributes.html) | Concrete connector fields and designated id/name defaults; schema replacement preserves settings for retained attributes and conditionally removes attributes absent from a newly discovered/uploaded schema when not used elsewhere. This supports dependency review, not universal schema-discovery or replacement behavior. Field names in InvoiceTrack are fictional, not Delimited File defaults. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Separate matching/assignment of account and identity attribute values under configured correlation. Both example accounts share a supplied employee reference and associate to one unambiguous existing identity; they don't merge into one account or receive missing account keys from correlation. Full criterion ordering, case behavior and reaggregation settings aren't taught here. |
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Identity-profile responsibilities and identity-value mappings differ from source-account schemas; identity attributes can map from source account attributes with supported transforms/rules. Adding an account field isn't an instruction to overwrite identity department or confer authoritative source responsibility. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Supporting established distinction between representing collected data and performing supported external account/access changes. Schema definition and audit evidence don't prove target writes. The core account-changing definition was rechecked on October 21; no new provisioning operation or tenant test is claimed. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

The two editable text diagrams match their inline counterparts after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 37 and 38 characters. The first is a read/mapping/representation relationship, not a private pipeline. The second depicts two supplied account-to-person associations, not merging or an invented matching score.

Employee number 44004 is shared by inv-acct-44004 and inv-admin-acct-44004. Their selected Account Name values are inv-44004 and inv-admin-44004; fullName = Devi Rao is non-designated descriptive data. I-44 is a non-runnable identity-ID stand-in. BLR is the supplied officeCode, 044 the textual costCentre and invoice-read/invoice-support the respective groups. The office schema definition exists while a wrong mapping reads missing siteCode; a fictional approved correction maps officeCode to office and subsequent supported collection supplies BLR. These facts are conditional teaching inputs, not observed tenant behavior.

The payroll transfer's duplicate local u17 keys belong to different people in two regional exports. Correlating them to different identities doesn't resolve conflicting account identification inside the proposed combined source. Separate supported contexts or deliberately designed stable keys can be acceptable design alternatives, not automatic repair/migration instructions.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| One employee, two accounts | A person key is automatically an account key | Preserve distinct supported account identifiers; use the separately configured employee-reference comparison for ownership. The supplied two accounts need not become two people. |
| Repeated descriptive full name | A duplicate friendly label proves duplicate account identification | Inspect actual selected Account ID/Name fields and values; fullName is non-designated in this example. It isn't permission to use repeated fullName as Account Name. |
| Office field defined but empty | Defining/re-adding a field supplies a missing or wrongly mapped value | Trace external officeCode, wrong siteCode input, represented office and subsequent collection. The supplied defect concerns mapping, not absent schema or unavailable external data. |
| Person reference without account key | Correlation manufactures identifiers | Investigate source data, read output and selected schema mapping under connector requirements. No universal reject/skip/overwrite result is asserted. |
| Account Name redesignation | displayAttribute means harmless cosmetic editing | Preserve documented post-aggregation risks; assess naming/ID dependencies and supported change handling rather than relabeling the designation casually. Primary schema reference above. |
| New schema field | Schema edit creates an external field and refreshes every existing account | Establish external value, read exposure, mapping and appropriate collection; external creation/update is a separate capability. No save-equals-reaggregate assumption. |
| Account department collected | Account and identity fields with the same English name are one value | Inspect identity-profile mapping and processing separately; account representation isn't automatic identity override or target provisioning. |
| Payroll-feed transfer | Regional key overlap can be fixed later by person correlation; surname is safe naming | Establish source boundaries and stable supported identification/naming with representative data; separate ownership matching; compare schema/mapping/read/dependency evidence and post-aggregation change risks. Accept supported separate contexts or a deliberately validated key design when applicable, not a universal concatenate/reset recipe. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## SRC-005: Attribute types, multiple values and data formats

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.5.

- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 45005, RouteBoard, route-45005, the API/file contract, faulty converter, approved correction and supplied reread are fictional. They do not describe ISC default conversion behavior.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: preserve value meaning, type, collection shape and transport format; distinguish missing states and date interpretation; trace configured conversions and consumers. No tenant configuration, provisioning request, complete transform payload, universal connector parser or private execution sequence is supplied.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Source attribute definition](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/sources/models/attribute-definition/) | Attribute name/type and separate isMulti, isEntitlement and related properties support the distinction between type, cardinality and access designation. A multi-value flag does not invent a string-splitting contract or make every collection an entitlement. |
| [Source attribute types](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/sources/models/attribute-definition-type/) | STRING, LONG, INT, BOOLEAN and DATE are model values. Whole-number representation differs from textual business codes. The lesson does not promise that every connector accepts arbitrary input formats or perform numeric-boundary configuration. |
| [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Schema/multi-value settings and the explicit constraint that Boolean attributes cannot be marked as entitlements. Schema definitions differ from collected record values; membership linking requires appropriate source/type/schema/identifier relationships. No universal null-clearing, deduplication or parser failure algorithm is inferred. |
| [Web Services SaaS response mapping](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/response_mapping.html) | Schema attributes map to response paths under the configured response/root. Wrong extraction can change selected value or shape. RouteBoard extraction is deliberately supplied as correct, with a later project-added faulty conversion. No exact reject/skip behavior for a wrong path is invented. |
| [Web Services SaaS response configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/configuration_for_response.html) | Nested response and collection extraction examples support checking scalar/list and object/identifier selection. Selecting one array element does not collect the whole array. This is not a complete connector configuration or arbitrary JSON-object serialization recipe. |
| [dateFormat transform](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/date-format/) | Documented input/output patterns and datetime conversion. The selected dd/MM/yyyy to yyyy-MM-dd example preserves the agreed 4 November 2026 meaning. Patterns are transform conventions, not universal connector field settings; formatting does not define termination time, scheduling or delivery. |
| [RFC 8259: JSON](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259) | Object, array, string, number, true/false/null, lowercase literals and transport representation. JSON has no native date type. Absent property, null, empty array, singleton empty string and literal text are structurally different; the standard does not define ISC operation semantics. |
| [RFC 4180: Common CSV format](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4180) | Informational description of comma-separated fields, quoting and escaping, not a claim that all CSV dialects are identical. The quoted Rao, Devi field remains one column. The inner semicolon membership convention is a fictional producer/import agreement, not an RFC rule, ISC default or asserted Delimited File configuration option. |
| [RFC 3339: Internet timestamps](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3339) | Numeric offset and UTC instant interpretation. 18:00 at +05:30 equals 12:30Z. A date-only value or one numeric offset does not supply every business cutoff or regional timezone rule. |
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Supporting identity mappings/transforms and processing boundary, retained from same-day course verification. A corrected collected account value does not prove a corrected identity value or resulting access delivery without checking consumers. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Supporting established distinction between reading/representing attributes and performing supported external account/access changes, retained from same-day course verification. No real target writes or tenant tests occurred. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text diagrams match their inline counterparts after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 35 and 40 characters. The first compares preserving a list with joining its elements, not changing external membership. The second is a human evidence/dependency trace, not an ISC private execution pipeline.

Employee 45005 uses account route-45005. costCentre is the significant textual code 007. groups contains route.read and route.dispatch as separate identifiers. The supplied bad project converter yields textual 7 and one combined membership item; the supported approved correction and reread restore the intended read-side representation without changing the external account or memberships. These are fictional facts, not connector defaults or observed test results.

The selected JSON block is valid and preserves strings, numeric quantity, Boolean true, a two-item array, date text and explicit null. The limited CSV alternative has four columns and one data row, preserves the quoted name and textual 007, and requires a separate field-specific membership interpretation. It is not a complete source snapshot/upload instruction. The date contract is day/month/year: 04/11/2026 means 2026-11-04. Offset arithmetic and the seven distinct missing/null/empty representations are checked independently. Invalid input must not silently become an assertion of no memberships; operation-specific error handling needs its own evidence.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Code 007 becomes 7 | A later text definition restores earlier lost information | Recover the original or use an expressly agreed formatting rule; do not invent padding from one record. |
| Text false | Every non-empty string means Boolean true | Establish supported parsing of the producer's convention and unexpected inputs. The faulty conversion is not ISC's default parser. |
| One joined membership item | Multi-valued means automatically splitting joined text | Inspect actual elements and preserve/extract/parse identifiers under the supported contract; brackets do not split strings. |
| Quoted comma in a name | All separators have the same scope | Parse quoted CSV columns separately from the fictional inner membership delimiter. |
| Omitted groups | Missing data proves no external access | Establish read scope, completeness, extraction and omission semantics; absence is not a successful removal operation. |
| Reformatted wrong date | Consistent output proves correct meaning | Honor agreed day/month order before formatting, and establish separate time/zone requirements. |
| Blank UI cell | Every blank-looking value should default to false | Inspect raw state, field type, business meaning and supported fallback conditions; false is a real value, not absence. |
| HR-feed transfer | One global conversion or null-clearing rule safely handles all fields | Establish field-level type/cardinality/format, preserve identifiers, define Y/N and inner-list parsing, check scope and missing-state semantics, specify date meaning/cutoff and verify raw-to-consumer evidence. No universal removal behavior is inferred. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.



## SRC-006: Entitlement membership attributes and entitlement schemas

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.6.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Official account-schema linking conditions, entitlement type/schema management, entitlement aggregation, entitlement metadata/permissions/deletion behavior and schema/attribute models freshly checked.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. SpendLedger Production, employee 46006, spend-46006, G-206, the mapping defect/correction, subsequent collection and every verification result in the scenario are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: account membership values, access-object details, source/type/identifier relationships, selected ID/Name, supported multi-type relationships, collection and UI evidence, safe schema changes. Complete aggregation execution, access catalog governance, requests/certifications and provisioning have their primary lessons. No configuration edit or target operation is performed.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Linking Aggregated Entitlements requires an entitlement-marked account attribute, attribute name identical to the entitlement type name, and collected value identical to the designated entitlement ID value. Entitlement and multi-value settings have separate purposes; multiple account attributes cannot use the same entitlement type. Schema changes have audit events. Fictional groups matches groups deliberately; the lesson does not prescribe renaming an existing connector's production schema or infer that a UI save reprocesses data. |
| [Creating and managing entitlement types](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/entitlement_types.html) | Supported sources/types and schemas, native-object-type naming, account attribute Type association, selected Entitlement ID/Name, initial first-attribute designation, post-aggregation duplicate risk, and supported entitlement-to-entitlement references. permission is specially handled and must not be invented as an ordinary type. Deleting a type does not delete associated entitlements; objects of that type cease aggregation/update until another type is created. SpendLedger groups/appRoles are fictional supported-native-type assumptions, not universal defaults or names to create on real connectors. |
| [Aggregating entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/aggregating_entitlements.html) | Separate access-object collection, direct-connect manual/scheduled reads, type selection and scope, manual flat-file imports and lack of multiple entitlement types through that flat-file mechanism. Catalog totals can include entitlements originating from account aggregation; inventory can exceed a single read's count. The lesson omits operational limits, schedule recipes and private queue details; no universal execution order or instant completion is promised. |
| [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html) | Catalog versus assignment, useful names/descriptions and owner/request/review context; supported direct/indirect permissions and nested relationships; schema-selected display attribute, mapping and manual-update evidence. Ordinary Display Name/Description edits do not write to the source and manually updated fields can be preserved against aggregation. Account aggregation does not delete entitlements simply because no account holds them. The lesson does not equate schema deletion, catalog editing or absent data with target revocation, nor promise all inherited/effective access is computed from names. |
| [Source schema model](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/sources/models/schema/) | Schema id is distinct from identityAttribute/displayAttribute, which name attributes selected for object identification/display. nativeObjectType identifies the external category; supported hierarchy representation is connector/schema-specific. G-206 is the fictional selected external identifier, not a runnable ISC record ID. No API payload, feature-flag modification or storage-key algorithm is taught. |
| [Source attribute definition](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/sources/models/attribute-definition/) | Separate type/schema association, isMulti and isEntitlement properties support distinguishing value shape, access designation and schema relationship. A collection alone is not access, and a flag alone does not establish correct collection/mapping/linking or external provisioning support. No universal coercion or arbitrary isGroup configuration is invented. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

The two editable text assets match their inline diagrams after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 33 and 44 characters. The first maps an account's groups G-206 reference through the same source/type to an access object and its details. The second compares the separate information supplied by account and entitlement reads and its linking agreement. Neither diagram asserts a private execution sequence, scheduling order, queue or guaranteed target write.

Employee 46006 is an expense reviewer. spend-46006 belongs to Expense Approvers in SpendLedger Production under supplied target facts. The raw selected account response has groups = [G-206]. The selected group record has groupKey = G-206, technicalName = expense.approvers, label = Expense Approvers and a meaningful description. groupKey/label are the selected ID/Name attributes. The deliberately faulty project mapping substitutes technicalName for the account reference. The supplied approved correction preserves G-206 and the subsequent verified result links the intended representation without changing target access. No product-default mapping failure or universal orphan/deduplication appearance is asserted.

A conditional supported extension uses appRoles R-51, Audit Exporter. Separate sources/types can reuse short code 51 without becoming one object. G-999 is an inventory-only case. The independent transfer uses teams T-8, teamCode and displayLabel Regional Reviewers; it rejects invented types or silent field merging to bypass the one-account-attribute-per-type constraint. Both selected JSON objects are valid explanatory fragments, not complete source payloads or change requests.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Matching label, different ID | Human-readable equality substitutes for selected identifier equality | Compare membership value and designated ID under the supported relationship; fix wrong mapping rather than casually redesigning existing identification. |
| Two phone numbers | Multi-valued means entitlement | Establish field meaning and supported access model; cardinality doesn't imply access. |
| Catalog-only group | Access-object inventory proves Devi holds it | Inspect account membership, identity association and any relevant indirect paths separately; do not invent a direct assignment. |
| Membership without description | Limited metadata proves invalid group or failed membership | Account aggregation can originate entitlements; check supported entitlement read, scope, mapping and upstream details rather than inferring target invalidity. |
| Reused short code across types | Equal text means one access object | Preserve source/type/value context and separate schema relationships. |
| Manual description update | ISC metadata edits provision source descriptions or must always be overwritten | No source write is implied; investigate manual-update preservation, mapping and collection evidence. |
| Deleted type | Schema deletion revokes everyone's access | It removes type/schema configuration and interrupts that type's collection/update; entitlement records and target membership are not thereby deleted. |
| Application-design transfer | Labels are keys, inventory equals membership, and two fields can share one type | Preserve T-8, connect supported teams/type/ID, separate label and membership evidence, and challenge the duplicate account-attribute/type association. Inspect native model instead of inventing a type or unverified merge. Establish access-changing support separately. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## SRC-007: Filters and collection scope

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.7.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Account collection modes, deletion safeguards/single-account limitations/history/Search freshness; connector SDK filtering; Okta SaaS inclusion filters; Entra ID account/group/membership scopes; entitlement aggregation, response mapping and API collection parameters freshly checked.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 47007, ServiceDeskFlow Production, all sample records, counts, read-actor visibility, query/paging evidence, defect/correction and subsequent results are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: decide intended coverage and interpret filter location/direction, read permissions, access scopes and completeness. Full aggregation execution, delta state, deletion threshold calculations, identity deletion analysis and connector syntax recipes have their primary lessons. No live filter, deletion setting or credential is changed.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Full/optimized versus supported delta reads, single-account operation boundaries, account deletion during full source reads, Enable Account Deletion and Percentage of Deleted Accounts Allowed safeguards, authoritative-account consequences, execution history and Search freshness. A full read remains bounded by configured source selection and visibility. The lesson does not prescribe threshold values, replay/delta tokens, private retry algorithms or production experiments. Full/partial and record/target changes remain distinct. |
| [Filtering using the connector SDK](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/typescript/filter/) | Native source filtering and resource-object-side filtering are distinct facilities; SDK evaluator/property types, comparisons and composite logic have a particular supported language. The lesson explains match direction as something to establish for the actual mechanism, not as a universal filterString inclusion/exclusion rule. No SDK recipe is asserted to work on every standard connector, and post-retrieval filtering is not claimed to reduce target read exposure. |
| [Okta SaaS supported aggregation filters](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/okta/help/saas_connectivity/okta/supported_agg_filters.html) | Concrete inclusion example status eq "ACTIVE" and combined AND/OR expressions. Its syntax and matching behavior belong to that supported setting. ServiceDeskFlow conditions are explicitly conceptual, not copied Okta defaults or a runnable ISC expression; case/null/invalid-input behavior requires the real mechanism's evidence. |
| [Microsoft Entra ID aggregation, filter and partitioning settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/microsoft/entra_id/help/integrating_entra_id/aggregation_settings.html) | Separate user, group and group-membership filters, and documented account-filter priority over group filters, including groups related to included accounts. This is a scoped connector example, not universal independence or precedence. No advanced-query flags, partitioning values, feature changes or syntax recipe is provided. Membership exclusion reduces read visibility rather than proving target revocation. |
| [Aggregating entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/aggregating_entitlements.html) | Access-object collection and type-specific scope differ from account and membership reads. Access-object inventory is not per-account assignment. The lesson does not promise all entitlement types or permissions from a completed account count. |
| [Web Services SaaS response mapping](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/response_mapping.html) | Root and response paths select represented input. Incorrect extraction/mapping must be separated from intentional population filtering. The lesson does not invent a universal skip/reject behavior or claim a schema definition supplies absent response data. |
| [Web Services SaaS aggregation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/json_aggregation.html) | Concrete supported response/paging configuration and cursor-based GraphQL pagination illustrate that completeness depends on the read's supported continuation. The 100-account/cursor scenario is fictional and not this connector's default page size. HTTP request success is not a completeness proof. |
| [Standard API collection parameters](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/standard-collection-parameters/) | API result filtering and pagination select the results of a read query, not the source aggregation configuration. The lesson does not copy endpoint defaults, maximums or query syntax into target/connector collection, or assume every API uses offset pagination. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text assets match the inline diagrams after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 31 and 38 characters. The first compares adding or omitting an ACTIVE inclusion condition while retaining WEST and HUMAN. The second is a human evidence-boundary trace, not a private ISC execution pipeline or a claim that every connector offers each separate filter.

The comparable supplied inventory contains 500 application accounts: 120 intended WEST human accounts plus 380 outside that agreement. Of the intended 120, 110 are ACTIVE and ten DISABLED. desk-47007 belongs to employee 47007, is WEST/HUMAN/DISABLED, and retains target memberships. desk-47008 is WEST/HUMAN/ACTIVE; desk-47009 EAST/HUMAN/ACTIVE; desk-svc-47010 WEST/SERVICE/ACTIVE. Initial onboarding assumes no prior ISC account record for Devi. The verified hypothetical actor visibility, completed pages, active-only selection, approved correction and 120-account reread are supplied teaching facts, not observed tests.

The simple predicates and counts are checked against the table. OR variations deliberately alter the boundary; a missing/unknown field's parser result is not invented. A separate first-page example contains 100 accounts and a continuation cursor. The payroll export is an independent transfer, with intended starters/recent leavers/unchanged workers missing from active-and-changed-today input. No real import, filter change, account deletion, access revocation or guardrail change occurred.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Equal total, wrong members | Matching counts establish coverage | Compare account identifiers and population categories; one omission and one unintended inclusion can cancel numerically. |
| WEST OR ACTIVE | OR retains the same region/human boundary as the agreement | ACTIVE admits the supplied EAST service account; re-establish all intended conditions and test representative cases under supported semantics. |
| Integration actor sees less | Administrator visibility proves integration visibility or bad business data | Compare destination, operation and actor authorization before editing region; no filter defect is inferred without evidence. |
| First page succeeds | One HTTP success establishes a complete population | Check continuation/terminal-page evidence, extraction and errors; the next page has not been shown as consumed. |
| Delta omits unchanged account | Missing change-set entry proves source deletion | Establish mode/change state and independent account evidence; a delta result isn't a replacement snapshot. |
| Viewing filter hides disabled account | Display absence proves collection absence | Clearing a read/view condition can reveal an existing record without changing collection settings. |
| Production filter drops half | Disabling safeguards validates an unexplained smaller read | Review intended exclusions, existing records, mode, completeness and deletion consequences safely; the guardrail isn't the cause of missing data. |
| Membership filter omits a target group | Collecting an account proves complete access visibility, or filtering revokes access | Separate account/membership scope and supplied target state; filtering changed represented information, not the external membership. Access removal needs its own supported operation/evidence. |
| Payroll-export transfer | Successful import makes a partial active/change file a complete replacement inventory | Establish intended status/effective-date coverage, export time and full/change contract, representative stable IDs and completeness. Review authoritative/existing-record effects; no automatic target-removal inference. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## SRC-008: Data ownership agreements

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 4.8.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Source ownership/account views, identity-profile mappings/required values/preview, account-schema change warnings, collection and identity processing, lifecycle-state boundaries, dateFormat, and attribute synchronization eligibility/direction/null handling freshly checked.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Worker 48008, PeopleLedger Production, GrantDesk Production, records, job times, stale view, correction and resulting target values are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: field authority, producer/integration/consumer responsibility, identifiers, requiredness, temporal and missing-value contracts, correction/acceptance/change evidence. These operating practices are an illustrative engineering model, not documented universal ISC roles, a contract-validation engine, automatic quarantine or automatic version arbitration. The sources below support product boundaries, not a mandate for the fictional team's job assignments.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Configured Source Owner, connection context and account views are real ISC concepts. Assigning an owner does not itself constitute the field-by-field business/producer/consumer agreement illustrated here. Source account views represent collected records, not proof that HR approved a value or a target changed. |
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative source/profile responsibility, cross-source identity mappings, supported transforms, required non-null User Name/Work Email/Last Name and unique User Name, plus the explicit distinction that Work Email is not validated as an email address. Preview inspects mappings with selected data; it is not external-delivery evidence. Project-required contractEnd is expressly not a universal ISC required identity attribute. |
| [Managing account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Schema definitions/designated uses versus record values; post-aggregation Account ID/Name changes are strongly discouraged, can break references or create duplicates, and Account Name is documented as immutable. Worker/person/target keys remain deliberately separate. No key migration, universal reconciliation winner or safe-rename recipe is invented. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Account collection and execution evidence are separate from producer correctness, identity calculation and external writes. A selected JSON fragment is not a complete authoritative import, and the fictional correction does not prescribe an unsafe reaggregation or deletion-setting change. Scope/completeness and existing-record consequences belong in the operating agreement. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Identity data is evaluated under configured profiles/access logic, with event/scheduled/time/manual mechanisms. Processing does not validate successful external provisioning after starting it. Modified time is evidence of an update, not proof of which field now meets the business requirement. No exact internal ordering, guaranteed cutoff delivery or schedule recipe is supplied. |
| [Synchronizing attributes](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/attr_sync.html) | Eligible account fields are included in Create Account definitions and directly mapped from identity attributes; synchronization applies to existing correlated accounts, does not create accounts, and enforces configured identity-to-account alignment. Null identity values from profile mappings can propagate; manual target deviations can be overwritten. GrantDesk expiryDate capability and configured mapping are explicit fictional assumptions, not claims about a real connector. |
| [Setting up lifecycle states](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/lifecycle.html) | Organizational state and enabled/configured actions differ from raw HR codes or date strings. The lesson establishes only this dependency; full state mappings, enabling states, account actions and removal behavior have primary lessons. No date text alone schedules an exact external action. |
| [dateFormat transform](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/date-format/) | Supported representation conversion is separate from approving a new fact or selecting business cutoff/time-zone semantics. No full transform payload or default-UTC claim is made. The next-day UTC boundaries are expressly supplied agreement choices and checked arithmetically. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text assets match the inline diagrams after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 30 and 37 characters. The first compares approved revision 18 (2026-12-31) with stale exported revision 17 (2026-11-30). The second is a responsibility/dependency handoff: business approval, producer correction, ISC values and verified target update. Neither is an undocumented ISC transaction or guaranteed execution schedule.

Devi is fixed-term project coordinator 48008. PeopleLedger workerKey PL-W-48008 identifies this worker record; personNumber 48008 is the separately agreed person reference. GrantDesk account grant-48008 is already associated to the same identity by supplied facts. managerNumber 48001 is a fictional manager reference, not a derived matching result. The selected corrected JSON fragment is valid; all other required identity inputs and account designations are explicitly assumed configured.

HR approves the extension on 20 November 2026 at 08:45 UTC. The export is generated at 09:20 and read at 09:30 the same day, but carries revision 17 because its view is stale. The producer's corrected publication supplies revision 18; the stated verified outcome is 2026-12-31 on source account, identity and target expiryDate. The existing target expiry feature and eligible configured synchronization are fictional supported-design assumptions. No future expiry execution or continuous sign-in was tested.

The agreement treats contractEnd as last authorized calendar day in UTC: 2026-11-30 maps to required boundary 2026-12-01T00:00:00Z, and 2026-12-31 to 2027-01-01T00:00:00Z. These are not product default meanings or proven delivery instants. Producer revisions do not give ISC automatic stale-row rejection, latest-arrival precedence, exactly-once processing or rollback. Business extension approval differs from correcting the stale export representation.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Later job, stale row | Delivery after approval proves delivery of current approved data | Compare identified row/revision/value, not just file time; producer correction is required in the supplied case. |
| Conflicting end dates | Choosing the later or last-read value is business authority | Establish field authority and direction, then trace the failed handoff; do not invent extra authorized time. |
| Missing usable email | Non-null placeholder satisfies a usable-address requirement | Distinguish ISC's required mapped value from business usability; follow approved missing-data handling without fabricated inputs. |
| Manual target exception | A target edit persists regardless of configured synchronization | Identity-to-account alignment can overwrite it; correct upstream cause and document authorized exception/reconciliation. |
| Null synchronized value | Blank always preserves target value | The documented configured sync mechanism can propagate identity null; agree operation-specific meanings and supported handling. |
| Older revision arrives later | Arrival time wins or revision field enables automatic ISC precedence | Apply expressly designed producer/integration ordering and recovery; no automatic protection is inferred. |
| Correct identity, denied target write | HR should change correct business data to fix permissions | Investigate supported target operation/input/authority, preserve approved value and verify resulting state after authorized correction. |
| Date formatted, boundary unknown | Correct representation defines expiry timing | Agree business day/hour/zone meaning, target representation and processing/delivery evidence; fictional UTC cutoffs are not defaults. |
| Laboratory-project transfer | One source owner, null/no-change, latest-arrival and green job replace a data agreement | Assign field-specific authority and handoffs, stable/manager identifiers, requiredness/status/missing semantics, scope/time/freshness/order, safe correction evidence and per-consumer acceptance. Preserve unresolved target outcomes. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

A read can supply data to later configured decision/change work, so the full workflow must not be described as universally consequence-free. No new account, membership or target field is changed in the main supplied result. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text assets match their inline diagrams after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 33 and 44 characters. The first depicts logical read dependencies, not separate private jobs, storage commits or a universal runtime host. The second separates the ReviewBoard-to-account HYD path from the HR-to-identity CHN path; it is not a write/synchronization instruction.

Devi 51001 is an operational case reviewer. ReviewBoard Production is non-authoritative. rb-51001 and devi51001 are the selected account key/name. employeeRef 51001 matches the configured identity employeeNumber under a supplied unique existing association; I-51 is a non-runnable teaching label. The account's operating-office representation changes BLR to HYD, while HR home-office CHN remains the configured identity value. G-51 identifies an existing case-reviewers group, unchanged by collection.

The selected raw JSON envelope contains one illustrative items entry with nested profile.siteCode HYD. The selected represented-attribute JSON has office HYD and preserves accountKey/login/employeeRef/groups. Both are valid, and neither is a full-population response or complete ISC accounts API object. Main-case paging/actor scope/identification and association outcomes are supplied, not real test observations.

Counterfactual first-collection, wrong branchCode path, employeeRef 51010, incomplete pages, unchanged-record optimization, downstream access and stale-file cases are explicitly variations, not simultaneous defects in the successful trace. The HR-record transfer uses an independent authoritative scenario. Formal learner testing has not occurred.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Different account/identity offices | Equal labels require equal values | Compare configured inputs and business meanings; ReviewBoard HYD and HR CHN can both be correct. |
| First collected representation | New ISC representation means a newly created external account | Supplied first collection creates the representation of an already existing external account; external creation requires its separate capability. |
| Wrong delivered path | Valid upstream value should be changed to fix a bad mapping | Compare profile.siteCode with wrong profile.branchCode, actual record and errors; no universal failure/clearing behavior is inferred. |
| Person-reference mismatch | Successful read proves correct ownership | Compare configured account/identity values and actual association; do not silently correct 51010 or match by office. |
| Unchecked continuation | First response success proves complete collection | Establish intended scope and supported page continuation to completion. |
| Changed criteria with unchanged data | Any new optimized run re-evaluates every account | Follow supported correlation re-evaluation and scope review; optimization can skip unchanged-account correlation. |
| Read followed by access change | Observation and membership-add operation are one action, or the whole workflow is harmless | Separate read input, configured decision and provisioning outcome; verify target state and acknowledge workflow consequences. |
| Newly delivered stale file | Calculation reconstructs current values missing from delivered input | Check producer source state and export selection; file time isn't row freshness. |
| HR-record transfer | One read guarantees identity establishment and downstream account creation | Verify source record/schema/scope/required values, authoritative profile and resulting identity, then separately configured creation/change capability and target result. Don't carry the non-authoritative ReviewBoard assumption into HR. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

### Official sources by claim and section

Changing ID/Name designations after aggregation can create duplicate entitlements. Supported object relationships can describe indirect access; no complete effective-access calculation is promised from two direct memberships. |

The correct source, account and association matter; a schema definition isn't evidence of delivered records or target membership changes. |

Devi's existing correct association is an explicit supplied condition; group inventory alone doesn't establish it. |

Observed membership isn't a newly issued grant. Collected data can feed subsequent configured change decisions, so complete workflows aren't declared universally consequence-free. |
