Revision 1.0. Documentation checked October 22, 2026. Documentation-based author review completed; tenant execution, beginner validation and formal publication approval not performed.

### Independent fictional evidence

- Devi is employee 76006. PeopleBridge Production is authoritative for Employees; pb-76006 already belongs to teaching I-76006, not an actual ISC ID.
- Applied eligible identificationNumber P-76006, email devi.rao@learn.example and name worker76006 identify Devi. Other I-76007 has P-76007/email other.worker@learn.example/name worker76007. No other candidate has P-76006.
- FlowRoom Production is non-authoritative for employee establishment. Account ID uses account key, Account Name uses login. fr-306/drao.fr/mail devi.rao@learn.example/personRef P-76006 is already automatically associated with Devi under the old criterion, according to supplied investigation evidence.
- fr-307/drao.review/mail obsolete.devi@learn.example/personRef P-76006 was evaluated under the old criterion and is supplied uncorrelated. Both accounts are approved for Devi; neither login matches an identity name and neither main account has manual treatment.
- Complete old list: mail Equals email. Complete replacement: personRef Equals identificationNumber, no email alternative. Both collected account records already contain the new reference and remain unchanged throughout the configuration comparison.
- Supplied optimized read skips both unchanged records. Supplied included unoptimized evaluation preserves fr-306-to-I-76006 and establishes fr-307-to-I-76006. Account keys/logins and identity are unchanged.
- Separate fr-309 is wrongly manually linked to I-76007 despite current P-76006; unoptimized aggregation does not clear the explicit link. Separate fr-310 was created through ISC and has manuallyCorrelated=true without a manual file import.
- Separate moved-account sync variation uses Operations on the wrong identity and Engineering on Devi, with department sync configured. The main example supplies no target write or access change.
- Project transfer population totals 400: 360 existing automatic links, 25 uncorrelated, 10 manual and 5 ISC-created persistent links. Fifteen persistent cases need separate review, not automatic release.

### Primary references

1. [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html): saved pairings/Equals/order/case, sample Test, searchable preparation, separate default naming path, persistent manual links, ISC-created manuallyCorrelated=true, unoptimized aggregation does not remove manual correlation, and supported correction contexts.
2. [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html): optimized unchanged-record skip without account/manager correlation, unoptimized recalculation, aggregation timing/status/scanned/mode history, manual-import filtering and Search freshness boundaries.
3. [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html): selected mappings, required identity attributes, Preview versus applied changes, profile processing status.
4. [Identity processing](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html): mapped attributes and configured access requirements, manual application, consumer effects and provisioning-success boundary.
5. [Account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html): Account ID/Account Name roles, immutable naming and serious reference/duplicate risks from changes after aggregation.
6. [Managing accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html): supported Update Correlation for uncorrelated or incorrectly associated accounts, including manual and aggregation-origin errors; account detail views.
7. [Viewing identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html): current profile/attributes/access and available events, not proof of historical criterion evaluation or independent business ownership.
8. [Attribute synchronization](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/attr_sync.html): configured existing-correlated-account writes, mapped attribute conditions and moved/correlated-account trigger when associated identity values differ; activity evidence versus external state.

All eight primary pages were fetched successfully during review. Correlation, identity processing, identity control panels and attribute-sync articles were read for their relevant full explanations; aggregation methods/activity and profile/schema/account-correction sections were read for the specific claims.

### Claim and scope checks

- Source criteria, account fields, identity mapping values and recorded relationships are separate objects/states. Saving criteria or seeing a sample estimate does not prove every existing link was updated.
- Account ID/key and Account Name/login designations stay fixed; criterion replacement is not a schema migration, account rename, identity creation or deletion.
- Main reference values predate the criterion replacement, isolating a configuration change. The old uncorrelated outcome is supplied with actual prior-evaluation evidence; no universal outcome is inferred from one comparison alone.
- Optimized unchanged-account reads skip account/manager correlation. Unoptimized evaluation is documented after correlation-config changes but is not guaranteed ownership repair, automatic unlinking/reassignment for every established automatic link, or manual override removal.
- Identity-side changes and application account-data changes are distinct. Preview, applied identity data and supported source-account evaluation require separate evidence.
- New custom fields require correlation-searchable preparation and manual processing for all identities before aggregation; Search queryability is a different capability.
- Manual relationships persist after account-data changes. ISC-created accounts may have manual-correlation treatment without file upload. A flag is not proof of human upload, ownership or approval.
- Current data/configuration compatibility is not a historical trace. Available history and events must not be enlarged into an undocumented candidate-by-candidate UI record.
- Removing configured email does not disable the non-configurable case-insensitive Account Name-to-identity-name default. No ambiguous-candidate tie-breaker is invented.
- An association move can trigger configured attribute synchronization; correlation is not access approval, target delivery proof or automatic reversal. Restoring old criteria does not demonstrate prior effects were undone.

### Claim and scope checks

- Employee account ownership and employee-to-manager relationships are different. Comparing the manager reference in account-owner criteria can select the wrong account owner; no such configuration change is performed.
- Manager Name is a mapping label, not a requirement to match friendly names. The account input is the employee's manager reference; the candidate field is the manager's own identificationNumber, not the employee's number or manager's manager reference.
- Both Manager Name mappings and source manager configuration are required. Matching can search identities established through other profiles; a manager reference does not create an identity.
- Profiles are considered by configured priority (lower number is higher); first non-null source is selected, not first successful candidate match. Main case has no competing mapped source.
- Null clearing is qualified by actual selected input, relevant evaluation and no competing non-null source. Changed unmatched non-null retains an existing manager under the documented behavior; the lesson does not infer a correct manager for an identity with none previously.
- Custom manager-matching identity fields require searchable eligibility unrelated to Search queries. Applied values, current inputs, configuration saves, Preview and actual relationship results are separate evidence.
- No duplicate-candidate winner, automatic cycle repair, fixed internal pipeline or guaranteed historical candidate UI trace is invented. Custom-rule behavior is a separate extension not present in the main case.
- Standard role/access-profile Manager review follows the access recipient, not automatically the submitter. Missing-reviewer handling belongs to request review and does not synthesize a manager. The lesson does not claim a universal fallback for every approval engine or automatic reassignment of existing tasks.
- The current standard missing-reviewer sequence was checked: missing reviewer's manager, next new escalation identity, fallback approver, requested object's owner, then eligible org admin, skipping already assigned approvers. Detailed approval-engine implementation remains in its own course topic.
- Manager Campaign exclusion/snapshot claims are scoped to that campaign type. A current corrected relationship is not proof of retroactive inclusion or reassignment; other certification modes need their own configuration evidence.
- Built-in Manager is not a direct sync input. A custom field alone does not establish correct manager-account selection, target representation, eligible sync or successful write. Main supplies no target operation.
- No actual tenant/API/source/profile/priority/mapping/correlation/processing/approval/campaign/synchronization/provisioning or target operation occurred. No destructive correction is recommended.

- Independent variations: explicit associated directory input Legal returns legal despite a PeopleLedger selector; FIN lowercases to fin without an approved translation; Finance with one trailing space retains that space after lower; null input has no approved finance fallback; custom accountAttribute default oldest Legal versus newer Finance needs active-worker selection design.

### Official sources and claim mapping

1. [Transforms](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/) supports configurable input/output objects, operations and nesting; implicit versus explicit input and override; separately configured account-create use. 3. [Lower](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/lower/) supports lowercase conversion and optional explicit input versus mapped source/attribute input. Mapped to Finance/FINANCE/finance, FIN to fin, Legal to legal and whitespace-preservation reasoning. No universal null/error outcome is asserted.
4. [Account Attribute](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/) supports account-associated value retrieval, default oldest-created selection, first-non-null behavior and optional sorting/filtering. 5. [Trim](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/trim/) supports separate removal of beginning/ending whitespace. Mapped to case 5's distinction: lower alone is not trim. No combined transform is implemented.
6. [Lookup](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/lookup/) supports key/value translation and optional default/unknown-key behavior. Mapped to why FIN needs an approved translation, not just case conversion; no unapproved lookup table is supplied.
7. [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) supports relevant event/manual processing, mapped identity updates, profile-associated Apply Changes scope and separate delivery validation. 8. ### Official sources, applicability and claim mapping

1. ### Source qualifications and review boundaries

- Lookup documentation's second example describes multiple input keys sharing an output but also includes an error statement about duplicate keys; displayed keys are distinct. This lesson does not repeat that inconsistent error explanation or rely on duplicate-output behavior. Its tables use distinct keys and outputs.
- No lookup case-folding, null-to-default, arbitrary object/list coercion or failed-read-to-default behavior is assumed. Non-null string matching/default paths are separated from these unresolved runtime contexts.

A [firsthand July 10, 2025 report of null/empty conditional failures](https://developer.sailpoint.com/discuss/t/conditional-transform-fails-when-attribute-is-null-or-empty/154131) reinforces the need to inspect actual retrieval and evaluation context; it is not proof of a universal empty-string runtime rule. Current operation documentation governs the stated null constraint.

Arrows describe value/branch dependencies rather than private service boundaries; lookup diagram begins after non-null string retrieval, and conditional diagram does not promise engine validation order.
- Two complete JSON definitions, two two-column tables, eight worked cases and transfer are retained. Local syntax/example/source synchronization checks are distinct from live rendering, accessibility and navigation verification. Prior lessons, tracing aid and UI code remain unchanged.

### Assessment mapping

- Case 1 tests key/value/destination and source-write distinction. Supplied FIN/table/mapping yields Finance without changing HR. Accept an explicit observation that application still needs evidence. Misconception: returned label writes back or root name selects destination. Sources 1, 3-4, 6-7.
- Case 2 tests business authority versus deployed configuration. Supplied RND/Research approval with unchanged table yields UNMAPPED, then controlled update and evidence. Accept correction of the producing feed instead only if the owner establishes the incoming code was wrong, which these facts do not. Misconception: HR approval updates a transform table automatically. Sources 1, 6-7 and approved example contract.
- Case 3 tests unmatched input without a default. ZZZ/no match/no default yields error, not guessed passthrough/blank/Finance. Accept a justified approved marker or explicit error policy as a future design, not the present result. Source 1.
- Case 4 tests successful wrong results. Finance worker plus wrong selected ENG and correct ENG/Engineering table yields incorrect business label through successful calculation. Investigate data/selection/collection, not corrupt valid ENG translation. Accept causal alternatives supported by additional evidence. Sources 1, 3-4.
- Case 5 tests false-branch meaning and independent task behavior. Intern unequal to Employee returns Manual review only. Misconception: exhaustive Employee/Contractor classification or task creation follows from output text. Source 2 and scoped owner agreement.
- Case 6 tests case sensitivity. Lowercase employee/no normalization selects Manual review; equivalence requires authority and implemented change. Misconception: equality silently ignores case. Source 2.
- Case 7 tests confirmed null versus insufficient visual-blank evidence. Null errors; another blank screen does not establish empty/null/space/error retrieval or a successful false branch. Accept request for exact input/evaluation evidence, upstream correction or implemented missing-data handling, not negative-branch error catching. Sources 2-3, 8.
- Case 8 tests calculation/application/display evidence. Collected Contractor/Preview Manual review with only historical current Standard review supports Preview result, not newly applied screen value or proof of failure. Need current identity and relevant processing/display evidence. Sources 6-7.
- Transfer tests generalization and classification authority. NYC/LON table and unexplained SGP make both proposed fabricated labels unjustified. Accept approved unknown marker restricted to suitable consumers, or explicit failure/correction design. Missing/null/error treatment must remain separate. Misconception: default/otherwise supplies an unproven business fact. Sources 1-2 and supplied facilities contract.
- Author review checks independent context, first-use meaning, supported mechanisms, causal variants, exact records/diagrams/answers and warm narration without learner citations or teaching-method promises. Actual beginner read-through and formal publication review/approval remain pending.

## TRN-006

Revision 1.0. Documentation checked October 23, 2026. Documentation-based author review completed; tenant execution, beginner validation and formal publication approval not performed.

### Independent fictional evidence

- Devi 86006 has teaching identity I-86006 (not actual ISC object ID), Employees profile and exactly one correctly associated AssignmentLedger Production account al-86006. Already collected assignmentEnd is the single string 10/23/2026 17:00:00 +0000, approved as a project assignment boundary on October 23, 2026 at 17:00 UTC. Other required fields valid; not an employment-termination scenario.
- Custom assignmentBoundaryReview is review-only, not access/status/account-action configuration. Approved outputs are Before boundary before the instant, Boundary reached at or after it.
- First JSON illustrates explicit accountAttribute sourceName/attributeName/return-first true under dateFormat inputFormat MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss Z, outputFormat ISO8601. Second JSON embeds the same retrieval/formatting as secondDate under dateCompare, firstDate now/operator gte/positive Boundary reached/negative Before boundary. The first standalone definition need not be saved or invoked separately; no intermediate identity attribute is implied.
- Expected interpreted instant is 2026-10-23 17:00 UTC; equivalent ISO representation with milliseconds and Z is shown, not a promise of exact tenant output spelling. Main supplied evaluations at16:59/17:00/17:01 yield Before/reached/reached.
- Third JSON is independent fixed static UTC input 2026-10-23T17:00:00.000Z to dateMath expression -24h. Arithmetic point is October22 17:00 UTC; documented short output 2026-10-22T17:00 needs adaptation before dateCompare. This is not dynamic retrieval, a reminder schedule or tenant-engine validation.
- Other numerical reasoning: 22:30 +05:30 equals17:00 UTC; 17:00 +02:00 equals15:00 UTC and precedes16:00Z; interpreting date-only October23 as midnight moves17:00 deadline17hours earlier; day/month March-text03/04 meansApril3 versus month/day March4.
- Supplied authorized processing at17:03 UTC establishes current custom label Boundary reached; no exact17:00 run is supplied. Collected HR unchanged, no employment/account/access/target-write or reminder outcome.
- Transfer supplies inclusive November1 as last permitted calendar day in unspecified partner zone. First excluded boundary is start ofNovember2 in that zone, subject to actual rules. No location-specific offset or DST transition date is invented; distinguish elapsed24hours from previous local calendar day.
- No actual tenant/API/source/profile/mapping/transform/aggregation/processing/provisioning or target operation performed. Local datetime/JSON checks validate illustrative arithmetic and structure only, not ISC runtime.

### Official sources, applicability and claim mapping

1. [Date Format](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/date-format/): parse/reformat by input/output patterns, named ISO8601 and epoch formats, explicit input and lack of now-keyword support. Mapped to first/nested JSON, format/epoch discussion and output-contract boundaries. No arbitrary timezone setting, default-zone guarantee or identical output spelling asserted.
2. [Date Compare](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/date-compare/): supported ISO dates/now, firstDate/secondDate direction, lt/lte/gt/gte and positive/negative output strings. Mapped to main definition, equality table and cases2-3. Null/malformed/retrieval errors are not classified as ordinary false outcomes or assigned an invented exact error text.
3. [Date Math](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/date-math/): UTC ISO input, component arithmetic/rounding, unit letters, now precedence, short yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm output and formatting before dateCompare. Mapped to fixed third JSON, -24h, rounding and composition warning. RoundUp false is explicit in prose example, not relied on as an omitted setting. No month-end/leap/locale/default-zone runtime behavior claimed.
4. [Account Attribute](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/): associated-account sourceName/attributeName retrieval and explicit accountReturnFirstLink true including null. Mapped to actual date provenance and one-account assumption; does not imply error suppression.
5. [Static](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/static/): fixed value output. Mapped only to literal UTC input for arithmetic illustration, not a substituted per-worker date or advanced template recipe.
6. [Transforms](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/): JSON root name/type/attributes, nested dependencies and explicit input. Mapped to definition interpretation and no automatic stored intermediates/private service pipeline.
7. [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html): custom attributes, mappings, selected Preview and shared application. Mapped to review-only custom field, Preview versus applied current evidence and shared impact.
8. [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html): processing updates, scheduled eligibility/queued execution, periodic refresh, per-profile nextProcessing/future ISO requirement/at-least-one-hour lead, and separate provisioning verification. Mapped to clock-passage diagram,17:03 supplied continuation and case8. Learner text avoids hardcoded schedule times/tenant-zone assumptions and arbitrary exact-time delivery claims.
9. [Java SimpleDateFormat](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/text/SimpleDateFormat.html): y/Y, M/m, d/D, H, s/S, Z/XXX and quoted literals. Mapped to pattern table, calendar versus week year, offset notation and literal-Z warning. Java reference supplies pattern meaning, not proof of every ISC parsing policy.
10. [Java DateFormat](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/text/DateFormat.html): parsing/formatting and configurable leniency. Mapped to distinction between successful parsing and approved calendar data; no exact ISC February30 rejection/normalization result asserted, and no Java setLenient method presented as an ISC transform setting.
11. [RFC 3339: Internet timestamps](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3339.html), sections4-5: instants/UTC/offset representation, ambiguous local times and restricted string-ordering conditions. Mapped to same-instant/different-clock examples and non-universal display-string sorting. RFC3339 is not treated as identical to all ISC ISO8601 accepted forms.
12. [Java ZonedDateTime](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/8/docs/api/java/time/ZonedDateTime.html), class introduction: zone rules mapping local clocks to instants, zero-offset gaps and two-offset overlaps. Mapped only to general zone/DST reasoning and local-boundary transfer, not a claim that ISC implements this class or its gap resolution strategy.
13. [RFC 8259: JSON](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259.txt), sections3-4 and7: objects, strings, booleans and null. Mapped to three parseable definitions and explicit true flags.

Current official cloud sources govern ISC claims; Java/standards references establish notation and time concepts, not undocumented ISC internals. Current product pages can use Human Fabric/Identity Security naming; course retains ISC. No IdentityIQ behavior imported.

### Scope and rendering boundaries

- Date-only value is not silently converted into an approved instant. Main input explicitly includes zero offset and time. No named-zone conversion recipe is offered without supported configuration/context verification.
- ISO representation is taught by contract rather than asserting every ISO variant is accepted. DateMath output omits seconds/offset; outer adaptation is required and described, not replaced by appending a literal Z or invented timezone attribute.
- RequiresPeriodicRefresh is explained as a periodic-reevaluation option, not inserted in a guessed configuration position or treated as exact-cutoff scheduling. Main examples do not configure scheduling. nextProcessing is supporting context; full scheduling/eligibility work remains Module9.
- Epoch choices are defined by origin/unit; producer-specific never-expires sentinels require independent documented meaning. No connector-specific sentinel values or execution recipe asserted.
- Diagrams: ../assets/TRN-006_date_input_format_and_comparison.txt and ../assets/TRN-006_boundary_reevaluation_and_applied_evidence.txt; exact inline copies, maximum widths41 and44characters. First describes value dependencies, not private services; second shows supplied evidence over time and explicitly denies exact-cutoff execution.
- Three full JSON definitions, two two-column tables, eight worked cases and local-end transfer retained. Local syntax/example/synchronization checks are separate from live complete-text, visual code/diagram/table and navigation verification. Prior lessons, tracing aid and UI code unchanged.

### Assessment mapping

- Case1 tests representation versus semantic correctness. Supplied day/month agreement and wrong month/day pattern produce April3 versus March4. Accept configuration/producer correction consistent with authority, not selecting a convenient interpretation. Misconception: parse success proves business correctness. Sources1,9,11.
- Case2 tests inclusive equality. Supplied equal17:00 instants/approved inclusive policy require gte; gt returns false. Accept mathematically equivalent reversed lte with corresponding operands, not gt unchanged. Sources2 and approved contract.
- Case3 tests operand direction. Supplied end17:00/now16:59 with end gte now returns reached too early. Accept corrected direction/operator preserving full statement. Misconception: operator name alone determines meaning. Source2.
- Case4 tests offset equivalence. Supplied17Z and22:30+05:30 are same instant. Accept explicit UTC arithmetic; no named-zone lookup is needed for supplied offsets. Misconception: local-clock difference implies elapsed-time difference. Source11.
- Case5 tests information loss. Supplied17:00 boundary reduced to date and reconstructed midnight shifts17hours early. Accept preserving full instant alongside separate display field. Misconception: shorter display preserves every consumer's meaning. Sources1,9,11.
- Case6 tests arithmetic versus output compatibility. Supplied fixed -24h and short returned string require supported adaptation/verified UTC before dateCompare. Accept source-based alternative composition respecting contracts, not direct string passthrough or cosmeticZ. Sources1-3,5.
- Case7 tests missing evidence and fallback authority. Blank display lacks actual retrieval/result; establish null/empty/space/error and approved absence policy. Accept explicit refusal to classify until that evidence exists. Misconception: distant-future default repairs missing deadline. Sources1-4,13 and scoped contract.
- Case8 tests time-dependent evaluation/application. Supplied16:59 stored label/17:01 observation/no later checked processing proves neither comparison failure nor exact-time application. Need processing eligibility/time/result/current value. Sources2,7-8.
- Transfer tests unfamiliar local inclusive date, zone authority and calendar versus elapsed arithmetic. Supplied throughoutNov1/partner zone requires approved first-excluded localNov2 instant under actual zone rules; warning lead needs elapsed24hours versus prior-calendar-day agreement, then evaluation/application/delivery evidence. Accept request for missing named-zone rules and supported conversion rather than numeric guessing. Sources1-3,8-12.
- Author review checks independent context, first-use definitions, source contracts, exact examples/diagrams/answers, causal changes and mentor voice without visible citations or teaching-method promises. Actual beginner validation and formal publication approval remain pending.

## TRN-007

### Independent fictional evidence

- Devi 87007 has teaching identity I-87007 (not actual ISC object ID), Employees profile and one correctly associated PeopleCore Production account pc-87007. Collected departmentCode is a single string with one ordinary space on each side of fin. Other required identity fields valid.

- Main inline JSON is root concat with ordered literal Department: plus one trailing space and lookup result. Lookup explicitly takes upper/trim/accountAttribute PeopleCore departmentCode with accountReturnFirstLink true; table includes UNMAPPED default. Trace raw spaced fin -> fin -> FIN -> Finance -> Department: Finance.
- Proposed shared Workforce department code is root upper with same explicit trim/account retrieval. It returns FIN, not Finance. Third JSON is full alternate concat/lookup with reference input attributes.id containing shared transform name. Pre-existing shared definition is an illustrative assumption, not an actual tenant-created object.
- Diagram's second proposed code caller indicates shared dependency only, not another authored tenant mapping. Each use has its own identity/account evaluation context; no shared cached value, saved intermediate field or private runtime order is assumed.
- Independent cases supply reversed concat (FinanceDepartment: with trailing space), outer uppercase (DEPARTMENT: FINANCE), spaced zzz -> ZZZ -> UNMAPPED, retrieval error, approved proposed shared output Finance breaking code-key caller, and separately associated DirectoryRoster dr-87007 department Legal under unchanged explicit PeopleCore retrieval.
- Supplied authorized caller/representative review, Preview and processing establish current I-87007.departmentReviewLabel Department: Finance. PeopleCore raw text unchanged; no saved fin/FIN/Finance fields and no external write/access/status/ownership outcome supplied.
- Facilities transfer supplies spaced lon, approved cleanup, LON/London table and Office prefix. Expected Office: London needs actual facilities input, not reuse of explicit PeopleCore department retrieval.
- No actual tenant/API/source/profile/mapping/transform/aggregation/processing/provisioning or target operation performed. Local parsing/value-reasoning checks are illustrative, not ISC engine execution.

### Official sources, applicability and claim mapping

1. [Transforms](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/): configurable JSON operations, nested returned-value dependencies, root-only names, explicit versus implicit input and distinct identity/account use contexts. Mapped to complete inline composition, structure/order explanations and actual source provenance. No undocumented execution service/order/intermediate-history/caching claim.
2. [Account Attribute](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/): associated-source sourceName/attributeName retrieval and accountReturnFirstLink true including null. Mapped to explicit PeopleCore inputs and one-account assumption; no error suppression or arbitrary-account selection inferred.
3. [Trim](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/trim/): surrounding-whitespace removal and explicit input. Mapped to raw ordinary-space code and empty-text distinction, not universal Unicode/missing/error normalization.
4. [Upper](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/upper/): uppercase string output and explicit input. Mapped to fin/FIN, finished-label uppercase variant and contract-specific approval. No global name-normalization recommendation.
5. [Lookup](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/lookup/): string key/value translation, configured unmatched default and explicit input. Mapped to FIN/Finance, ZZZ/UNMAPPED and code-versus-label contract changes. Null/failed retrieval/configuration errors are not assumed unmatched strings; no unverified automatic input cleaning/case-folding.
6. [Concatenation](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/concatenation/): ordered values array of literal strings or nested returned results. Mapped to exact Department prefix, final assembly and reversed-array case. Literal punctuation/space is supplied explicitly.
7. [Reference](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/reference/): reuse existing transform logic, attributes.id is the pre-existing transform's name, optional input and result as if logic were inline. Mapped to full alternate definitions, per-context results, source-bound versus input-driven helper distinction and dependency/caller review. Not a generic object-ID lookup, captured prior identity value or instantaneous population update.
8. [First Valid](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/first-valid/): first non-null configured result and distinct error-continuation policy. Mapped only to broad-fallback diagnostic caution, not a supplied fallback recipe or omitted-default claim.
9. [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html): custom attributes, mappings and selected Preview/shared application. Mapped to custom review field and preview/current-state distinction. Multi-value input requires actual mapping/representation verification, not invented generic list coercion.
10. [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html): relevant identity reevaluation/application and separate provisioning verification. Mapped to supplied continuation and shared-edit evidence limits.
11. [RFC 8259: JSON](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259.txt), sections3-5 and7: objects, arrays, strings, booleans/null and ordered arrays versus named object members. Mapped to parseable definitions, operation versus data distinction and property/values-order comparison.

Current official cloud references govern technical claims. Product pages can display Human Fabric/Identity Security naming; course retains ISC. No IdentityIQ behavior imported.

### Scope limits and rendering boundaries

- Main data contract is one non-null code string and owner-approved normalization. Missing, empty, multi-value, wrong-account and error inputs require their own verified handling; no one-chain promise for all representations.
- Source-bound helper deliberately includes PeopleCore accountAttribute. Reference's optional input does not justify claiming an outer selector replaces every deeper explicit read. No untested generic implicit-input reference recipe provided.
- Wrong/unavailable referenced names are configuration dependencies, not ordinary unknown codes. Cyclic dependencies are excluded as design requirements; no exact recursion error, evaluation algorithm or supported object-rename operation claimed.
- Lookup documentation's unrelated duplicate-key/output example contains inconsistent error wording. No duplicate-output or omitted-default behavior is imported into these definitions, which have unique keys and explicit defaults.
- No universal store, cache, log or job exists merely because intermediate values are drawn in a diagram. Source definitions and actual evaluation evidence must establish context; copied instructions and shared references have different maintenance consequences.
- Sources: ../assets/TRN-007_nested_value_dependencies.txt and ../assets/TRN-007_shared_definition_and_callers.txt, exact inline copies, maximum widths36 and45characters. First is returned-value dependency trace; second is caller-definition dependency map, not shared cached result flow.
- Three full JSON definitions, two two-column tables, eight worked cases and facilities transfer retained. Local source/JSON/example checks are distinct from live complete-text, visual/accessible code/diagram/table and navigation checks. Prior lessons, tracing aid, UI and tests unchanged.

### Assessment mapping

- Case1 tests instructions versus values. Supplied upper/trim/accountAttribute chain and raw spaced fin produce FIN for lookup, not JSON object data. Accept full value trace. Misconception: nested operation objects are the raw business value. Sources1-5.
- Case2 tests array order and literal characters. Supplied Finance result and reversed two-item concat yield FinanceDepartment: with final space; no separator is inferred. Accept exact equivalent character explanation. Misconception: concat/list order behaves like arbitrary object property order. Sources6,11.
- Case3 tests operation placement. Supplied whole-label uppercase returns DEPARTMENT: FINANCE, violating approved case. Accept identifying that root placement changes input scope rather than claiming upper malfunction. Sources1,4,6.
- Case4 tests successful unknown marker versus valid meaning. Supplied spaced zzz/ZZZ and explicit default yield Department: UNMAPPED, no real department established. Accept investigation of approved translation/data despite successful assembly. Sources3-6 and scoped contract.
- Case5 tests failure versus normal unmatched input. Supplied failed account retrieval/no error handler does not establish ordinary default/final result. Accept inspection of actual failed evaluation and approved policy, not guessed null/empty/retention/error class. Sources1-2,5.
- Case6 tests shared output contracts. Supplied Finance-return proposal and unchanged code-key caller yield unmatched marker after evaluation, even if edit is approved. Accept coordinated caller redesign preserving approved final meaning. Misconception: approved shared change is automatically compatible. Sources5-7.
- Case7 tests explicit provenance through references. Supplied DirectoryRoster Legal selector and unchanged PeopleCore retrieval use HR code; require actual saved-reference evidence. Accept shared-definition inspection, not selector-only inference. Sources1-2,7.
- Case8 tests scope/application evidence. Supplied saved reference and selected Preview establish only that context, not all callers' stored fields or external delivery. Need caller/record coverage, relevant processing/current values and separate consumer evidence. Sources7,9-10.
- Transfer tests generalization without source/meaning confusion. Facilities spaced lon/approved normalization/LON London/Office display yield Office: London, but PeopleCore source-bound helper retrieves wrong subject matter. Accept properly designed input-driven reuse with verified context/error contract or distinct facilities definition. Misconception: matching operation names prove interchangeable business inputs. Sources1-7 and facilities agreement.
- Author review checks independent context, first-use definitions, supported operation contracts, exact records/values/diagrams/answers, failure boundaries and personal mentor narration without learner citations or teaching-method promises. Lookup has ENG/FIN keys and no default. Supplied diagnostic observation establishes unmatched-key failure for spaced ENG; outer expression returns Finance. ### Official sources, applicability and claim mapping

- Custom assignmentReviewStatus independently reads associated account reviewAt and compares evaluation time with it: before returns Before review, equality/after returns Review due. Review-only metadata has no employment, account-disable, access-removal, email or notification action.
- Recurring-only design: explicitly UTC tenant, no roles implemented, no account for Devi on a sync-configured source; Active identity; periodic requirement marked true; no nextProcessing. Therefore morning sync-account selection absent, evening no-role/periodic-profile selection present.
- October23 recurring-only observations: initial configuration application/currentBefore13:00, selectedPreviewDue/currentBefore both checked16:01, eligible evening queued20:00/start20:04/currentDue after relevantprocessing20:06. Initial application is not claimed to be morning scheduled processing. Source timestamp unchanged.
- Alternative design, not the same execution history: per-profile nextProcessing mapping receives approved available source timestamp independently from review calculation. Applied identity nextProcessing16:00 verified after13:30update gives2h30lead; relevant processing/currentDue verified16:02. Fictional, not an exact-time/two-minute SLA.
- Too-close first identity update15:30 for16:00 gives30min, even if HR approval09:00. Lead is from nextProcessing identity attribute update in ISC, not HR's producer time, a proposed Preview or just the date text.
- Explicit offset11:00-05:00 equals16:00Z; no inferred local named-zone offset. ReviewAt postponement18:00 with an earlier16:00 evaluation correctly Before, but needs a verified revised next-event plan. No automatic recurring reset, past-date recovery or clearing/retention behavior asserted.
- Case8 supplies separate offboarding identity stage verified but target disable error; target account state unverified/unsatisfied, not hidden by processing success.
- Transfer: business wants09:00 per employee's location from date-only input and periodic option; need approved local zone/instant, recurrence versus per-identity plan, lead/application and separate email mechanism if required.
- No actual tenant/API/source/profile/mapping/aggregation/processing/provisioning/notification or target operation performed. Local date/offset/lead calculations are illustrative arithmetic, not ISC runtime/tenant scheduling tests.

### Official sources, applicability and claim mapping

1. [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html): scheduled08:00/20:00 tenant-zone population rules, defaultCST/CDT, queued scheduled start, per-profile nextProcessing/future ISO/one-hour lead from identity update, additional versus recurring processing, configuration application and provisioning completion limit. Mapped to both designs and timing investigation. No unverified time-based internal queue architecture, universal identity eligibility or exact-second target-delivery promise.
2. [Date Compare](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/date-compare/): now at evaluation, ISO input, directed inclusive comparison and periodic-refresh option. Mapped to independent Before/Due rule and time-dependent truth versus stored result. No operation JSON or flag-placement recipe supplied; periodic marking is not a minute timer.
3. [Account Attribute](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/): selected associated-account field input. Mapped to independent account reviewAt input with one account, not a fresh live producer read or implicit peer-identity calculation.

[Identity Attribute](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/identity-attribute/): explicit restriction on using peer identity-field reads during concurrent identity-profile calculation. Mapped to independent nextProcessing/review inputs and no list-order readiness assumption; no such unsafe JSON prescribed.

Mapped to proposed versus applied nextProcessing, initial configuration application and current review-label evidence.

No universal time-based eligibility restriction inferred from scheduled eligibility alone.

Mapped to explicit11:00-05:00/16:00Z arithmetic and date-only versus exact-moment contract. Not a claim that every syntactically valid RFC3339 variant is accepted by every transform.

Current official cloud documentation governs technical claims. Some pages use Human Fabric/Identity Services names; course retains ISC. No IdentityIQ behavior imported. Source availability is checked separately from correctness and learner validation.

### Scope and rendering boundaries

- Documented recurring population table is necessary to explain selection, not an exhaustive identity-state lesson. Main Active/no-roles/periodic/no-sync/UTC assumptions are explicit before relying on them.
- Scheduled jobs are queued at configured times and can start later. Per-identity16:00 is a requested processing time with supplied16:02 observed result, not an asserted identical queue implementation or delay bound. Diagrams are conceptual responsibilities.
- NextProcessing requires applied identity evidence and sufficient lead. Profile-level mapping save and Preview are not equivalent. Earliest-next-milestone planning is design reasoning, not a copied fixed expression or promise ISC automatically advances/resets dates.
- Revised reviewAt and scheduled nextProcessing have different meanings. An earlier run can legitimately return Before against postponed boundary, leaving a later evaluation requirement.
- DateCompare/equality reasoning is independent from scheduling. The review label has no configured external consumers; the security cutoff discussion deliberately requires separate target state and authorized delivery design.
- Editable diagrams ../assets/PRC-003_clock_rule_and_applied_value.txt and ../assets/PRC-003_recurring_and_time_based_processing_paths.txt exactly match inline text; maximum widths42 and42characters. First separates clock rule/stored result; second compares recurring and additional per-identity responsibilities.
- Two two-column tables, eight worked cases and local09:00 reminder transfer retained. Verify complete live body, exact accessible diagrams/tables and native navigation separately from local time/source checks.
- Existing processing comparison guide and attribute tracing guide remain revision1.0 unchanged. PRC-004 reserved/planned only, no file or new navigation module. Beginner validation and formal publication approval remain pending.

### Assessment mapping

- Case1 tests rule versus recurring timing. Supplied UTC/no-role/no-sync/Active/periodic design, correctPreview/currentBefore16:01 means evening path not yet due, not necessarily comparison failure. Accept need for actual execution and approved latency. Sources1-2,5-6.
- Case2 tests evening no-role profile eligibility. Supplied Active/no-role/no periodic field does not establish inclusion in that documented population. Accept identifying missing selection condition and appropriate timing need, not inventing another trigger. Sources1-2,6.
- Case3 tests representation versus lead requirement. Supplied validISO16:00 firstupdated15:30 gives30min, not requiredhour. Need supported recovery/future plan. Reject formatting as override of lead. Sources1,9.
- Case4 tests producer versus identity application time. Supplied HR09:00/ISC15:30 means one-hour lead measured from identity update. Accept tracing collection/mapping/application to diagnose delay. Misconception: early HR approval establishes ISC scheduling readiness. Sources1,5.
- Case5 tests instant/representation distinction. Supplied explicit11:00-05:00 and16:00Z same date identify same instant. Accept arithmetic normalization and refusal to freeze offset for future local dates. Sources2,9.
- Case6 tests queued/start/applied evidence. Supplied20:00/20:04/20:06 is consistent with delayed scheduled execution, not transform arithmetic failure; business acceptability still independent. Accept timing-gap investigation without target outcome inference. Sources1-2.
- Case7 tests corrected business boundary versus prior plan. Supplied reviewAt18:00/run16:00 returns Before correctly. Need applied revised plan for later evaluation; unchanged answer is not skipped-calculation proof. Sources1-2,5.
- Case8 tests identity stage versus failed delivery. Supplied current stage/target disable error cannot close cutoff; authorized correction and actual target verification needed. Accept identifying missing actual disable evidence; reject date or processing status as target-state proof. Sources1,6-8.
- Transfer tests per-location business timing versus tenant recurrence. Supplied date-only/local09:00/periodic option needs approved zones/instants and accepted delay or valid applied nextProcessing with lead, plus updated future plan and independent email delivery if required. Accept supported alternative design meeting explicit business timing; reject one tenant job as every person's local timer. Sources1-2,5,7-9.
- Author review checks independent context, first-use terms, population/lead/time conditions, exact values and alternative histories, diagrams/answers, missing-evidence discipline and mentor voice. Actual beginner read-through and formal publication approval not performed.

- Existing processing comparison guide and attribute tracing guide remain revision1.0 unchanged. PRC-005 reserved/planned only, no file/new module. Beginner validation and formal publication approval pending.

## ENT-001

Revision 1.0. Documentation checked October 24, 2026. Documentation-based author review completed; tenant execution, beginner validation and formal publication approval not performed.

### Independent fictional evidence

- Devi employee100101, InvoiceDesk Production/accountiv-100101, businessUnitWest, enabled account and successful sign-in. Correct account-to-identity association is supplied, except case7 explicitly varies it. No actual ISC object ID is invented.
- Supported account/group reads and correct schema linkage are supplied. Account membership attribute groups is Entitlement/Multi-Valued with Typegroups; native entitlement type groups. Entitlement schema selects id for Entitlement ID and displayName for Entitlement Name. Account groups[G-4101] matches the group idG-4101/InvoiceReaders. These selected teaching records are not a required connector payload, API shape or default configuration.
- Owner-supplied application policy: G-4101 permits invoice reading within accountbusinessUnit only, no editing/approval. G-4102/InvoiceApprovers permits approval within businessUnit but does not itself release payment. No claim G-4102 membership alone permits every action or that Readers/Approvers are universal SailPoint permissions.
- Initial actual application results are supplied: West readallowed, East readdenied, approvaldenied. They illustrate expected authorization boundaries, not a live authorization engine simulation. No unsupported app permission discovery or ISC per-invoice decision implied.
- Cases are independent alternatives: emptygroups/no otherreadgrant; existingnativegrant/noISCrequest; ownerconfirmedaccidentalediting; Testcatalog/Productionaccount; Readers-onlyapprovaldenial; twentyholders/oneshareddefinition; wrongidentitycorrelation; verifiedG-4101removal with G-4199independentWestreadinggrant retained.
- Transfer ProjectReviewernativerole/R-82/proj-501, correctlycorrelated supportedroleentitlement, assignedprojectreportread only/noedit, requestabilitynotconfigured. No specific real connector or same-system ISC role assumed.
- No actual tenant/API/source/schema/aggregation/provisioning/application policy change, grant, revoke, sign-in or target operation performed.

### Official sources and claim mapping

1. [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html): source access rights, supported entitlement types/permissions, display and owner metadata, direct requests, profiles/roles and certification use. Mapped to the initial entitlement meaning, metadata/evidence table and supporting governance connections. No entitlement represents a universal single atomic permission, no arbitrary permission type created and no catalog-to-holder inference.
2. [Loading entitlement data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/index.html): account membership representation versus separate object-detail collection; supported full catalog can include unheld access items. Mapped to collection explanation, diagram1 and cases1/6. No claim entitlement aggregation alone changes account membership or creates target groups.
3. [Creating and managing entitlement types](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/entitlement_types.html): supported native object type, schemas, selected ID/name and account entitlement-attribute Type linkage. Mapped to groups/groups fictional contract, id versus displayName and identifying-value match. Not all connectors support these capabilities; exact native type is not an arbitrary label.
4. [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html): schema roles, Entitlement/Multi-Valued designations and linking collected membership values with entitlement ID. Mapped to schema explanation and diagram1. Both account attribute and native type are groups here, satisfying the account-schema page's same-name condition as well as the entitlement-type page's explicit Type configuration. No contradictory general assertion that any differently named field automatically links.
5. [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html): account-to-identity relationship. Mapped to the independent identity/account distinction and case7. Account correlation does not change native group membership or prove approval.
6. [Authentication versus authorization, Microsoft identity platform](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/authentication-vs-authorization): supporting general distinction between establishing identity and deciding permitted actions. Mapped to diagram2 and invoice behavior. InvoiceDesk's group/businessUnit policy is wholly fictional, not a claim that Microsoft or SailPoint enforces it.
7. [Configuring entitlement access requests](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_entitlements.html): requestability and applicable approval configuration are separate from collecting entitlement data. Mapped to existing native grants, requestability and transfer. No blanket approval requirement or claim all catalog items are requestable.
8. [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html): same-source entitlement bundle and standing versus JIT activation distinction. Mapped to brief profile explanation and limited JIT caveat. No detailed detection/removal algorithm, automatic grant from creating a definition or unactivated-access usability implied.
9. [Managing roles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/roles.html): access profiles/direct entitlements, configured assignment and standing/JIT behavior. Mapped to ISC access-model role versus a source-native role. Dynamic dimensions and full role design deliberately deferred.
10. [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html): external access changes, automatic/manual fulfillment and configuration dependence. Mapped to approved requirement versus actual delivery, read/write capability boundary and verified selected membership removal. No configured write assumed from read support, no unconditional retry or literal internal queue.
11. [Certifications overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/certs/index.html): assigned reviewers assess selected access for appropriateness and retention/removal. Mapped to the short governance explanation. No claim all represented access is certifiable or decision automatically proves completed revocation.

Official cloud documentation may use Identity Services or Human Fabric names; the course retains ISC. No IdentityIQ mechanics imported. Source references are author-only, absent from the learner body.

### Scope and rendering boundaries

- This lesson teaches what an entitlement represents and how to reason about its holder and native permission effect. Detailed identifier management is reserved for10.2, catalog freshness10.3/10.6, inheritance10.4 and sensitivity/ownership10.5. Necessary supporting meanings are still explained in place.
- Two editable text diagrams exactly match assets/ENT-001_account_membership_and_entitlement.txt and assets/ENT-001_application_authorization_boundary.txt, each maximum40columns. Diagram1 is relational, not a claimed execution schedule. Diagram2 is an expressly supplied fictional application decision path, not ISC backend internals.
- Two two-column tables cover policy meaning and evidence limits. Eight cases have explicit Facts and Reasoning; one different-system transfer supplies its own object, membership, scope and requestability conditions.
- businessUnit is an authorization scope input here, not automatically an ISC entitlement. A group may carry several actions, and an account may retain a capability through a separate supplied grant.
- Removal of a shared definition is not prescribed as account revocation. No actual delete behavior, nested membership traversal, runtime timings or broad access-removal guarantee claimed.
- Keep learner source links/course links absent. Preserve prior72lessons, all previous assets including both reference aids, templates/CSS/tests/specifications. Module10 is added only with this complete lesson. ENT-002 is planned without a stub.
- Verification covers authored consistency, preservation, source URLs, navigation and exact deployed article/diagrams/tables, not tenant execution or beginner learning outcomes.

### Assessment and alternative reasoning

| Item | Understanding tested | Misconception or unsupported alternative rejected |
|---|---|---|
| Case1 | Shared object versus account membership, with fresh empty collection and no other grant supplied | Catalog existence grants access to Devi |
| Case2 | Native grant provenance versus represented holder and policy judgment | No ISC request means no real entitlement or automatically inappropriate access |
| Case3 | Confirmed native policy governs editing; controlled application correction needed | Display name/description can remove editing or outweigh current evidence |
| Case4 | Source/environment context identifies the relevant access object | Matching display names prove production membership |
| Case5 | Expected permission boundary versus missing delivery/new requirement | Repeat Readers provisioning to obtain approval |
| Case6 | One access definition with many holder relationships | One catalog item per member is required |
| Case7 | Account correlation versus membership and access meaning | Rename/describe group to repair wrong identity association |
| Case8 | Confirmed selected removal versus broader remaining capability | Any surviving read proves that removal failed, or all other access may be removed without review |
| Transfer | Native role/ISC role, supported reference/holder, resource scope and separate requestability | Every source role is an ISC role; denial always means broken delivery; every catalog item is requestable |

Author review checked first-use meanings, person/account/group identifiers, correct conditional scope, source/type/ID/name linkage, exact diagram equality and narrow width, case independence, cause-based correction and source placement. Real beginner read-through, teach-back and transfer validation remain pending.

Revision 1.0. Documentation checked October 24, 2026. Documentation-based author review completed; tenant execution, beginner validation and formal publication approval not performed.

### Independent fictional evidence

- Devi employee100202, correctly associated RecordsVault Production accountrv-100202/groups[G-6202]. Source S-102, schema SC-102 and entitlement E-102 are expressly short teaching labels, not real tenant IDs or valid API values.
- Supplied supported native type groups, account entitlement attribute groups/Multi-Valued/Typegroups; schema identityAttributegroupKey and displayAttributelabel. Selected sourcegroupfields groupKeyG-6202, technicalNamecase.review, labelCaseReviewReaders, descriptionReadassignedcasefiles/noediting.
- Owner verifies same G-6202 object retains key, members and assigned-case-read/noedit permissions while label changes CaseReview to CaseReviewReaders. Current ISC record E-102/sourcevalueG-6202/newlabel and fresh accountmembership independently verified as example facts. No assertion that every connector rename preserves IDs or refreshes all views immediately.
- Selected model field meanings: idE-102, nameCaseReviewReaders, attributegroups, valueG-6202, sourceSchemaObjectTypegroups, sourceS-102. No full API payload, request schema or endpoint version asserted.
- Independent variations: APIexpectsISCentitlementID; verifiedrename; deletedG-6202/replacementG-9202 with no newmembership and oldpackagereference; invalididentityAttributeG-6202/no suchfield; displaytechnicalNameversuslabel correction; supportedgroups42versusroles42; protectedmanualdescription; changedowner for futureconfiguredowner-reviewrequests.
- License transfer has supportedlicenserepresentation, nativeLIC-17, ISCteachingE-L17, identityfieldlicenseCode/displayfieldtitle, ReportEditor-to-ReportAuthor with samecapabilities/key/record/holding verified. SeparateLIC-29 has differentfeatures and needs its own requirement/references/fulfillment review.
- No actual tenant/API/source/schema/aggregation/provisioning/metadata/owner/application permission or target operation performed.

### Official sources and claim mapping

1. [Entitlement model, official Angular SDK](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/entitlements/models/entitlement/): distinct id/name/attribute/value/sourceSchemaObjectType/source fields, source attributes, accessModelMetadata and manual-update map. Mapped to record-context explanation and metadata distinction. These are selected model meanings, not a complete payload, API request shape, universal UI labels or a claim all fields are required.
2. [Schema model, official Angular SDK](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/angular/sources/models/schema/): schema id, nativeObjectType, identityAttribute and displayAttribute. Mapped to schema-record ID versus selected field versus individual object's value. No identityAttribute/person-correlation equivalence.
3. [Creating and managing entitlement types](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/entitlement_types.html): supported native type, schema ID/name selections and duplicate risk after changing them; account Type linkage and primitive-versus-related type distinctions. Mapped to types, relational diagram and controlled identification/display change. No unsupported custom type inferred from a friendly category and no guarantee display changes are risk-free.
4. [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html): entitlement attribute designation, multiplicity and linking source values with selected entitlement ID. Mapped to accountgroups[G-6202] and same-source/type contract. Both account attribute and native type are groups here, satisfying same-name and explicit Type linkage conditions together.
5. [Managing entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/entitlements.html): represented access, names/descriptions/owner/requestability, supported source collection and manual-field protection. Mapped to metadata purposes, source versus locally maintained display text, stale-description investigation and case7. No actual manual-update property spelling imposed; document prose and interface model can differ. No universal metadata-to-native-permission write.
6. [Loading entitlement data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/index.html): separate account memberships and entitlement details. Mapped to collection explanation and value-to-object relationship; no label collection implies membership creation.
7. [Managing access model metadata](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/metadata.html): managed default/custom metadata for access objects. Mapped to organization-defined context versus collected source attributes and native permissions. No arbitrary custom classification treated as an enforcement rule or required project feature.
8. [Configuring entitlement access requests](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_entitlements.html): requestable items and configured entitlement-owner review. Mapped to request setting and future owner-routing case. No claim a changed owner receives the entitlement or already-created requests automatically reroute.
9. [Managing access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) and [Managing roles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/roles.html): same-source profile bundles, role access groups and entitlement references. Supporting replacement-impact questions and distinct access-model type meanings. No bulk migration algorithm, removal propagation or broader assignment behavior assumed.
10. [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html): account-to-identity association. Mapped to the brief independent supporting definition; not confused with identifying an entitlement object.
11. [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html): changing external access and source/configuration dependence. Mapped to reading a changed label versus delivering a new membership. No real grant or guaranteed connector-write support asserted.

Official cloud documentation may use Identity Services or Human Fabric names; course retains ISC. SDK source models are primary reference definitions, not IdentityIQ implementations. Source references remain author-only.

### Scope and rendering boundaries

- Primary coverage is identifiers, display selections, types and metadata. Full catalog reconciliation/freshness is reserved for10.3/10.6, inherited access10.4 and sensitivity/ownership design10.5. Supporting explanations remain self-contained.
- Two editable text diagrams exactly match assets/ENT-002_identifiers_schema_and_membership.txt and assets/ENT-002_verified_rename_and_replacement_boundary.txt, maximum40/39columns. First is relational, not a hidden backend sequence. Second compares supplied verified before/after records, not a universal rename-state guarantee.
- Two two-column tables compare values/record jobs and metadata/evidence limits. Eight cases each supply Facts and Reasoning; license transfer changes object kind while preserving the reasoning task.
- Qualified source/type-or-attribute/value is an investigation discipline, not an asserted internal database uniqueness/deduplication key. No permanent/reusable native identifier or cross-environment ISC ID portability promise.
- Schema-field selection changes are not a workaround for friendly labels. Display correction still requires supported controlled verification; ID/name changes can duplicate records. Native-group replacement cannot silently migrate references/memberships.
- Manual description protection and maintenance authority are distinguished from failed collection. Owner/requestability can affect configured processes, while description/classification does not itself rewrite target permissions.
- Keep all prior73lessons, assets/reference aids, templates/CSS/tests/specification byte-equivalent. ENT-003 remains a plan without a learner stub. Working edition has74lessons/10modules.

### Assessment and acceptable alternatives

| Item | Understanding tested | Misconception or unsupported alternative rejected |
|---|---|---|
| Case1 | Receiving operation determines identifier kind; actual retrieved ID needed | Nativevalue can replace ISC ID; teaching labels are valid tenant inputs |
| Case2 | Verified same-object rename retains supplied membership/permissions | Any new name demands another grant |
| Case3 | New native object and old stored reference require approved replacement review | Same label automatically migrates package references/memberships |
| Case4 | Schema field selector versus one record's identifying value | identityAttribute should contain G-6202 |
| Case5 | Display need versus identification contract; both require controlled verification | Choose label for ID too because it is readable, or display changes are universally harmless |
| Case6 | Source/type context distinguishes equal raw values | Rawvalue42 alone identifies the right access item |
| Case7 | Manual-update protection and intended metadata authority | Old text proves source read never occurred or repeated aggregation must overwrite it |
| Case8 | Configured future-owner routing versus native access/existing work | Ownership is purely cosmetic, grants access or automatically reroutes old requests |
| License transfer | New display value versus distinct license/capability replacement | Newtitle implies newgrant, or LIC-29 is equivalent solely because replacement is desired |

Author review checked independent context, first-use meanings, field/value/record distinctions, supplied continuity evidence, conditional source behavior, case/diagram agreement, narrow width and no body citations/meta-announcements. ## ARC-005

- Lesson: [ARC-005](../lessons/ARC-005_Connection_Dependencies_and_Operation_Support.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 3.5.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026; current official VA network/proxy requirements, source/SaaS definitions, connector capability/authorization examples, account schema, provisioning evidence and primary HTTP semantics references reviewed. Supporting IQService, identity/account/access-profile and source-correlation meanings retain earlier checks on the same date.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 35005, OrderDesk, orders-35005, svc-order-isc, orders.export, Orders-Prod, source/cluster/VA names, audit and network incidents, and ResearchArchive are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: operational dependency reasoning and evidence across actual runtime paths, not a full network/protocol course, connector setup runbook, universal API error dictionary, or private platform execution algorithm.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [VA system and network requirements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/requirements_va.html) | Relevant runtime's DNS, time synchronization, required cloud and target connectivity, target-specific ports and private-CA/inspection trust conditions. The lesson doesn't freeze changeable endpoint/IP/port lists or treat cloud connectivity as a target test. |
| [VA configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/config_va.html) | HTTP proxy route includes applicable source HTTP/HTTPS traffic; actual configuration and supported exceptions matter. Proxy admission differs from target admission. No proxy credentials, bootstrap commands, bypass changes or unrestricted firewall opening are prescribed. |
| [VA deployment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/deploy_va.html) and [management](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/manage_va.html) | Source-to-cluster/member execution and health scope retained from ARC-002. Main member selection is supplied evidence, not a fixed worker algorithm. A different VA or laptop check isn't substituted for the actual member. |
| [Sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Configured source versus connector, VA versus SaaS mode, and read-only versus write-capable options. A source label or successful read doesn't create capability, destination correctness or target authority. |
| [SaaS connectivity](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/connectivity/saas-connectivity/) | Hosted connector runtime and no customer VA for that supported path; cloud-hosted business application doesn't by itself select mode or supply private API reachability. No generic direct-cloud inspection tool is assumed. |
| [IQService introduction](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/intro.html), [prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/system_requirements.html), and [TLS/client authentication](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/common/topics/configuring_tls_and_client_authentication_for_iqservice.html) | Supporting operation-specific Windows leg, source/client/process credential roles, separate trust and client registration boundaries verified in ARC-004. IQService isn't used in the main OrderDesk path. The alternate case supplies corrected TLS followed by an unregistered-client refusal; no universal check order or full handshake is claimed. |
